  - `connection inspect` advised `init --connection <a connection that keeps no records>` when the tracking tables are on another connection; it names the tracking connection now;
  - the `retail` README said that `fct_shipment_packages` and the calendar do not render for PostgreSQL: all 23 models validate, deploy, refresh and match DuckDB there.
- **Found and documented, not changed**: a SQL Server database created with the server's default collation makes `connection status` report DDB-310 for view columns made of string literals (the READMEs say to create it `COLLATE Latin1_General_100_CS_AS`); the string profile is parked. Smaller points (wording of `status` for copies, the history question for a full-replace table, repeated DDB-302 notes, `inspect` not listing the schemas of mapped models) are in OPEN-ITEMS section S.
- **Tests**: unit tests over every template now also run `model update --check`; new ones for the refresh plan of `stores` (`excluded`), the expansion of `macros`, the enum refusal, the date literal, and `model update` with a copy. Conformance (`Group=templates`): `macros` joins the other templates on both engines (every mart against DuckDB's rows), and `StoresTemplateConformanceTests` runs `stores` on four databases (rows per shop on both engines, the report on both engines against the origins' rows grouped by the test, `compare` between the engines, a refresh that leaves the copy alone and a deploy that picks up a new order).
- **Verified on a real engine**: SQL Server 2022 and PostgreSQL 17 in docker, by hand and by the tests: `retail`, `chinook`, `adventureworks`, `stores` and `macros` through compile, seed, init, status, deploy, refresh and monitor; the `refresh.check` levels `none`, `project`, `objects` and `live` on `retail` (SQL Server); `plans.deploy.keep: database` applied by id, `refresh.check: live`, `refresh.check: project` with `on_fail: warn` and `keep: ephemeral` (SQL Server); `audit: full` (SQL Server, a refresh); `compare` between SQL Server and PostgreSQL for `stores`. Test results: unit 1861 passed; conformance `Group=templates` 15 passed (the 10 template builds on both engines, 4 with the rewrites off, `stores`), the rest of the suite 173 passed and 9 skipped (Oracle, Spark, BigQuery, SQL Server 2025 and scale tests need their engines).
- **Not verified**: **Fabric** (no template or sample has ever run there); the lifecycle settings of `chinook` and `adventureworks` on PostgreSQL (they were uncommented on SQL Server only; the settings do not depend on the engine); the `live` check on PostgreSQL ran once with nothing changed (it found nothing); the terminal, web and MCP interfaces over the new templates; incremental copies in `stores` (not part of it); a `NAME` parameter per connection in a template.

## 145. Title, description, tags and properties on every object; tracking layout 6 (OPEN-ITEMS section T, first half)

- **Built**: `title`, `description`, `tags` and `properties` (a mapping of key to one value) on the project (top of `dbdatabuild.yml`), each connection, each model, each column and each index; mapped and native models, their columns and their indexes take them too (DESIGN.md 6.5.8). `ObjectInfo`/`PropertyValue` (`DbDataBuild.Models`), one reader (`YamlFieldReader.ReadInfo`) for all of them, and the old inline tag check of the model loader now goes through it. Property values are text, number or boolean, decided by how they are written (`true`/`false` unquoted are booleans, plain digits are numbers, everything else and anything in quotes is text) and held as text with a type tag; in the metadata documents they are JSON strings, numbers (the digits written) and booleans. Limits: title 200 (one line), description 4000, tag and property key 128, property value 4000; a new diagnostic, DDB-243, for a limit or a key or tag of the wrong shape (a value of the wrong kind is DDB-106); a property key may not end in `-`, `=` or `+` (they are the merge suffixes).
- **Layering**: `properties` joined `LayeredKeys`, so it merges down `defaults:` of `dbdatabuild.yml` and of each folder file with the nearest winning, `properties=` replaces, `properties-: [key]` removes keys (the same code as `tags`). `title` and `description` are not layered: either one in a `defaults:` section is DDB-104. The project's and a connection's own words inherit from nothing.
- **Documents**: `title`, `description`, `tags` (sorted) and `properties` (sorted) are in the project document (and in each `config.connections` entry), the model and source documents and on each of their columns and indexes, and are left out when empty, so a project that says nothing has the same documents byte for byte (the rendered goldens and every hash are unchanged). `schemas/metadata.schema.json`, `model.schema.json`, `source.schema.json` and `config.schema.json` have the keys. The source writer writes them back (so `import --refresh` keeps what a person wrote, and `SourceImport.ToDescriptor` carries the committed descriptor's words, those of its columns and of its indexes); the definition hash of a source is made without them.
- **Tracking layout 6**: the table `object_info` (connection, subject_kind, subject, element, item, item_key, value_type, value, recorded_utc). `connection publish` (and `metadata.store_on_apply`, the same code) deletes and re-inserts the rows of a subject whose document changed, rows first and the document after, 50 rows to an insert statement through the tracking gate; a subject that stops saying anything has no rows left. `init` creates the table (a layout 5 store lacks only that table, so `init` brings it to 6 without altering anything, and records 6 only when the table is there); `init --upgrade` from 5 does the same after its steps; a layout 5 store seen by commands is DDB-505 and the message says `init --apply`. `tracking_version` is read as before.
- **Selector**: `property:key=value` (the value as text, whatever its type) and `property:key` (has the property), small enough to build: one more line in `ProjectContext.ResolveCore`, in the help text and in the docs. A value with a comma cannot be selected.
- **Rules over metadata**: `metadata_info` (the same rows as `object_info`) and `title`, `description`, `tags` on `metadata_models` and `metadata_sources`, so a project test can say "every mart has a description and an owner" (`templates/starter/tests/metadata/marts_are_described_and_owned.sql`).
- **Used in templates**: `starter` (project, connection, two marts with columns and an index, folder files giving the layer tag and property) and `stores` (the four connections); both READMEs say what it is. SKILL.md, `docs/operations.md`, DESIGN.md 6.5.8, 9.4, 9.4.1, 9.8, 9.9 and 12 are updated.
- **Not built** (OPEN-ITEMS section T): the same four on foreign keys, tests (a test keeps its `description` and `tags` as comments), hooks, loads and parameters; a model's query head (`.sql`) cannot carry them; writing them into the database (a later decision, as decided).
- **Tests**: unit `ObjectInfoTests` (45 cases: types of values, every kind of damaged input as a diagnostic with a position, the limits at their edges, a diagnostic that names the folder file, layering with every suffix, the selector, the documents, the rows extracted from them, the writer round trip, the import refresh, the metadata views), a DDB-243 fixture, `FuzzTests` now mutate files that carry all four keys, the tracking DDL test (6 and the version-recording condition). Conformance on SQL Server 2022 and PostgreSQL 17: `The_words_about_objects_are_stored_as_rows_beside_the_documents_and_replaced_when_they_change` (rows for the project, a connection, a model, a column and an index with the value types, a second publish writes nothing, a changed description replaces one model's rows and no other's, saying nothing leaves none) and `A_store_of_layout_five_lacks_only_the_info_table...` (refused, `init` creates the table and records 6, `--upgrade` the same, repeatable); the layout 3 and 4 upgrade tests and the old-layout test now end at 6.
- **Verified on a real engine**: SQL Server 2022 and PostgreSQL 17 in docker. Results: unit 1907 passed; conformance `Group=apply` 124 passed and 3 skipped (two failed the first time on a stale expectation of layout 5 and passed once corrected), `Group=templates` 15 passed, the quick group 51 passed and 6 skipped (engines not started), `Group=release` 4 passed.
- **Not verified**: **Fabric** (the `object_info` DDL is generated for it like the other tables and has never been run; the layout upgrade is refused on Fabric as before); a store with a very large number of rows in `object_info` (the primary key is wider than SQL Server's 900-byte clustered key guidance, as `metadata_document`'s is; the real values are short and nothing failed); the terminal, web and MCP interfaces do not show the words yet.

## 146. The positional connection name (OPEN-ITEMS section T, second half)

- **Built**: `connection <verb> [connection]` for `inspect`, `init`, `status`, `deploy`, `refresh`, `monitor`, `compare`, `seed` and `publish`; `project import [connection] [tables]` and `ui terminal [connection]` the same. The connection is the first positional argument and is optional: left out, it is the project's only default connection (`CommandTargets.Resolve`, unchanged), and with several defaults the message says to name one after the command (`connection status sqlserver`). **`--connection` is removed from them, with no alias**: a value that looks like an option in a positional place is refused, naming the new way (without that guard the parser takes `--connection` for the connection's value and runs on the default connection with `--connection` as a model selector). Unknown names are still `Unknown connection ...`.
- **A command that takes models, tables or patterns after the connection** (`deploy`, `status`, `refresh`, `publish`, `import`) reads its first argument as the connection when it is one of the project's connections (exact name, case counts), and otherwise as the first model, table or pattern: `connection status marts.orders` works in a project with one connection. A name that is both a connection and a directory of models is the connection. A selector that names nothing and looks like a plain name now adds the project's connection names to its message (a mistyped connection is the likely cause). `compare` keeps the connection first: `connection compare [connection] <table>` (with one argument, it is the table).
- **Kept as options on purpose**: `project compile --connection` and `project model create --connection` (a list of connections, a filter) and `project show plan --connection` (a filter of the plans listed). They are not "the connection a command works on"; say so if the owner wants them positional too.
- **Interfaces**: the MCP tools and the web page's calls take the argument `connection` as before (the JSON property is the same, now a positional that is put first on the command line); the terminal form has a `connection` argument field (prefilled with the session's connection) and no `--connection` field; the generated command reference shows a `Usage:` line per command; `next` hints and header text print `connection init sqlserver --apply`. The skill, `docs/*.md`, the template READMEs (`stores`, `retail`, `chinook`, `adventureworks`: their sample outputs now say 14 statements and layout 6, which the new tracking table makes true but which were not re-run), DESIGN.md and the design notes are updated; the progress log's older entries are history and keep `--connection`.
- **Tests**: 113 calls in the unit and conformance suites moved by a script (the connection after the verb; read through), and the text expectations by hand. New `PositionalConnectionTests` (19 cases): the old option is refused by each of the eleven commands and nothing runs, the tree has no `--connection` on them and the connection is the first optional argument, optional with one default connection and required with two, the split rule, `compare` and `import` with and without the connection, the hint and header text, the terminal form and the MCP tool schema and argument order. Conformance on SQL Server 2022 and PostgreSQL 17 runs every command of the real loops in the new form.
- **Verified on a real engine**: SQL Server 2022 and PostgreSQL 17 (docker). Results: unit 1926 passed; conformance `Group=apply` 126 passed and 3 skipped, `Group=templates` 15 passed, the quick group 51 passed and 6 skipped (Oracle, Spark, BigQuery, SQL Server 2025 and scale tests need engines that were not started), `Group=release` 4 passed.
- **Not verified**: **Fabric**; the terminal interface was not driven (`scripts/tui_drive.py` needs `pyte`, which is not installed here): the form is covered by `FormModel` and catalog tests only; the web page was not opened in a browser (its calls are covered by the tool-surface tests); the stores README's sample lines were edited, not re-run.

## 148. Refresh plans, step 1: plans, memberships, load plans, `--input`, selectors, views, tracking layout 7 (OPEN-ITEMS section U)

- **Built** (design `docs/research/refresh-plans.md`, section 12 says where the build chose): `refresh:` in a model, and in `defaults:` of the project or a folder file (layered like `tags`: a list appends, `refresh=` replaces, `refresh-: [plan]` removes), as `plan: load`, `plan: { load, as, inputs, on_failure }` or a list; `refresh: { plans: { hourly: { title, description, tags, properties, on_failure, on_busy, expected_every, parallel, check, on_fail } } }` in `dbdatabuild.yml`. A model with a default load and no `refresh:` is in `default`. New codes DDB-244 (a plan the project does not declare), DDB-245 (a membership name twice), DDB-246 (a load or input the model lacks, or a value of the wrong type), DDB-449 (a load that needs a person in a plan, or run without its value), DDB-450 (no such plan); DDB-446 and DDB-448 now speak of the compiled files.
- **Compiled files**: `rendered/<c>/loads.yml` (every load plan of every model, with `project_hash`, `shape_hash` per load and `excluded`) and `rendered/<c>/refresh/<plan>.yml` (members in dependency order, each naming a load plan by hash, with inputs). Deterministic, hashed, covered by `project compile --check`, written only by a whole compile; `refresh.plan.yml` is gone and a compile removes an old one.
- **Commands**: `connection refresh [connection] [plan] [selector ...] [--input [model.]name=value]` (selectors: the model grammar plus `load:`/`exclude:load:` and `*`/`?` patterns; a load named `model.load` runs alone; the old "default operation only" behaviour is gone); `project show refresh [plan] [--connection c]`; `connection monitor [connection] [plan]` (runs, complete and partial apart, each member's last success and failure with duration); `connection status` prints a line per plan. Header and Next hints name the plan; JSON documents and `schemas/output.schema.json` (closed) updated, `model.schema.json` and `config.schema.json` too; `docs/commands.md` regenerated; SKILL.md, concepts, operations, DESIGN.md (6.x and 10.6), the `stores` and `retail` templates (`hourly` and `daily`; `stores` copy stays left out with its reason) and READMEs updated.
- **Tracking layout 7**: `migration_log.refresh_plan`, `selection`; `run_log.membership`, `load_plan_hash`, `inputs`, `attempt` (1), `duration_ms`; the table `refresh_plan_member`. `init` creates the table, `init --upgrade` adds the columns (SQL Server and PostgreSQL; refused on Fabric as before); the layout is recorded only when the last column is there. Decision: `run_log.inputs` is a new column even though `parameters` already held the sources.
- **Tests**: unit 1942 passed (new `RefreshPlansTests` 14: declaration, damaged files, layering, DDB-244/245/246/449/450, selectors, input precedence, the command up to the connection, `project show refresh`; `RefreshPlanTests` and others updated). Real engines, SQL Server 2022 and PostgreSQL 17 in docker: `Group=apply` 130 passed and 3 skipped (new: a plan by name, a selection, a load named directly with `--input` recorded as `flag`, the same load twice under two names; layout 6 to 7 upgrade on both engines; the layout 3, 4 and 5 upgrade tests moved to 7), `Group=templates` 15 passed, the quick group 51 passed and 6 skipped (engines not started here), `Group=release` 4 passed.
- **Not verified**: **Fabric** (never run; layout 7 DDL generated only); the terminal interface and the web page were not driven; `expected_every`, `on_failure`, `on_busy`, `parallel` are validated and stored but do nothing yet (step 2 onward); plans on PostgreSQL were exercised by the same conformance test as SQL Server, the templates' new plans only through compile and (stores) refresh of `daily`.

## 150. Refresh plans, step 2: failure rules, a lock per load, `--only-failed`, `max_runtime`, `expected_every` (OPEN-ITEMS section U)

- **Built** (design `docs/research/refresh-plans.md`, section 13 says where the build chose): `on_failure` (`stop`, `continue`, `ignore`; plan, membership overrides) with dependents found through the project graph; per-load states `done | failed | skipped | not run` written as `run_log.status` text (`ok`, `failed: check | max runtime | busy | input`, `skipped: upstream failed | busy | stopped | check failed`, `not run`), a row for every selected load; outcomes `completed | completed_with_failures | stopped | failed` in `migration_log.status` and the JSON `outcome`/`results`/`warnings`; exit 1 unless completed, with a summary line. A failed refresh check on an object is a load failure of kind `check`. `connection refresh <plan> --only-failed`. A lock per load (the connection's lock shared, the load's exclusive, SQL Server `sp_getapplock` / PostgreSQL advisory locks, session locks) with `on_busy: skip | wait | fail` and `max_wait`; a busy skip is not a failure and what reads it runs, marked `upstream skipped: busy`; a deploy that cannot get the whole connection exits 4 (DDB-439). `max_runtime` on a plan or a load (`loads.<name>.max_runtime`, in the load plan hash): statement cancelled, transaction rolled back, load `failed: max runtime`. `expected_every`: `OVERDUE` and `SLOW` (naming the slowest load) in `connection status` and `connection monitor <plan>`, `health` in their JSON. New plan settings `max_wait`, `max_runtime`; spans accept `s`. `config.schema.json`, `model.schema.json`, `output.schema.json`, `docs/commands.md`, DESIGN.md, concepts, operations and the skill updated. **No layout 8**; no new diagnostic code.
- **Changed behaviour**: a refresh refused by the check now leaves an event (`failed`) with the loads `failed: check`; the monitor keeps an unfinished refresh as "needs attention" until a later one completes (the JSON conformance test now runs a refresh after accepting drift).
- **Tests**: unit 1947 passed (new `RefreshRulesTests`: spans, status texts, plan and load settings, the shared/waited lock). Real engines, SQL Server 2022 and PostgreSQL 17 in docker: `Group=apply` 138 passed and 3 skipped (new, on both engines: failure policies and dependents, `--only-failed`, the check as a load failure, two refreshes at once with a slow load and all four `on_busy` modes, a deploy kept out with exit 4, `max_runtime` with rollback and the lock released, `expected_every` overdue/slow), `Group=templates` 15 passed, the quick group 51 passed and 6 skipped, `Group=release` 4 passed.
- **Not verified**: Fabric (never run); the terminal interface and the web page were not driven for `--only-failed`; `parallel` is still only stored; a membership has no `max_runtime` or `on_busy` of its own.

## 151. Refresh plans, step 3A: inputs and resolvers (OPEN-ITEMS section U)

- **Built** (design `docs/research/refresh-plans.md`, section 14 says where the build chose): `inputs:` in a load (`type`, `default`, `resolver`, `on_null`, `provides`; `schemas/model.schema.json`). Resolvers: `expression` (DuckDB in a fresh in-memory database: external access off, extensions off, configuration locked, 256 MB, 10 s; the clock functions answer one frozen instant in the zone `timezone:` says, UTC by default; `timezone:` is new at the project and on a connection, validated, `schemas/config.schema.json`), `target` (a `SELECT` over the project's models, lowered, rendered for the engine into `rendered/<c>/<model>/input.<load>.<input>.sql`, run through `ReadSession`, two rows read at most, a 600 s timeout) and `source` (the same for a copy, one file per origin engine, run at the origin on its read login); `provides` (one resolver, several inputs by column name); `on_null: fail | initial | skip_load`; a value converted to the type without loss (DDB-453 names the input, the load and the resolver kind). Inputs resolve in dependency order; a circle is DDB-452; a resolver that does not compile (a table, a parse error, an unknown name, a construct the engine lacks) is DDB-451. A flag or a membership value replaces the resolver, which is not run. A refresh resolves each load just before it (`failed: input` for DDB-453, `skipped: no value` for `skip_load`); the event records every input with its source (new: `default`, `resolver:expression`, `resolver:source`), the resolver's hash and its duration, and `run_started`. A deploy resolves the inputs when it plans (a set per origin for a copy; the load step per origin) and keeps them, with how to compute them again, in the plan file and its report. A range load of a copy gives its types with `params:`.
- **Tests**: unit 1969 passed (new `InputExpressionTests` 6, `InputResolversTests` 16: declaration and damaged text, `timezone`, compile, the rendered file and its hash, parameters, DDB-451/452, the engine of resolution with a fake connection, the planner per origin and the plan file's round trip). Real engines, SQL Server 2022 and PostgreSQL 17: `Group=apply` 142 passed and 3 skipped at this commit (new, on both engines: an expression on a frozen clock, a `target` resolver, a group, an override that skips the resolver, `skip_load`, NULL with `fail`, a value that does not fit; a `source` resolver of a copy run at the origin, in both directions).
- **Not verified**: Fabric (never run); the terminal interface and the web page.

## 152. Refresh plans, step 3B: the moved-input rule of a deploy (OPEN-ITEMS section U)

- **Built**: `connection deploy` shows each computed input when it plans (screen, plan file, report) and takes `--input [model.load.]name=value` (planning: a pin, not computed, so it cannot move; `--apply-plan`: the deployer's explicit yes, with that value). `apply` computes every input again (the declared ones, the watermark); if any differs it stops before anything runs (DDB-454, exit 1), prints each moved input with the planned and the new value and each override as it would be typed, and records nothing; an interactive apply asks once ("Continue with the new values? [y/N]"); `--yes` does not approve; the planned value is recorded beside the bound one in `run_log.parameters` (`Planned`). The old refusal for a moved watermark (DDB-437) is replaced by the same rule. `SessionInputRunner`/`OriginInputRunner` live in `DbDataBuild.Apply`. `schemas/output.schema.json` has the new fields (`inputs` of a plan step, `inputs` and `moved_inputs` of the deploy document).
- **Tests**: unit 1973 (new `InputMovedTests`: the printed override, the flag's grammar, a malformed `--input`). Real engines: `Group=apply` 146 passed and 3 skipped at this commit (new, on both engines: the plan shows the window; midnight passes and the apply stops with both values and the overrides, `--yes` stops too, the JSON document has `moved_inputs`, the overrides appended to the same command load those values and record the plan's; an interactive no, then yes; a pinned input cannot move while the other one does).
- **Not verified**: Fabric; the terminal interface and the web page for the new option and the prompt.

## 153. Refresh plans, step 3C and D: `--resolve`, `--dry-run`, the templates, the documents (OPEN-ITEMS section U)

- **Built**: `project show refresh <plan> --resolve` (the expression resolvers evaluated offline on one instant in the connection's zone; the ones that read a database are named as not resolved here); `connection refresh --dry-run` runs the `target` resolvers read-only and prints `input <name> = <value> [<source>]` per load and `resolved_inputs` in the document. `retail` has a `reconcile` load on `fct_sales_lines` with an expression window (`days` 14, 30 in `daily`) and its README explains it; `fan_in_fan_out` declares no resolver, and its README says why (a window load would remove the other origin's rows of the same days; a single-origin copy is covered by a conformance test). DESIGN.md 6.6.3, concepts, operations, SKILL.md, `docs/commands.md`, schemas, `refresh-plans.md` section 14, OPEN-ITEMS U and `lifecycle-implementation.md` updated.
- **Tests**: unit 1974 passed. Real engines, SQL Server 2022 and PostgreSQL 17 in docker: `Group=apply` 148 passed and 3 skipped; `Group=templates` 15 passed (retail now also runs `refresh daily` and the whole history through `reconcile --input days=4000` before the marts are compared with DuckDB); the quick group 51 passed and 6 skipped (engines not started here); `Group=release` 4 passed.
