## 154. Refresh plans, step 4: copies as loads in a refresh (OPEN-ITEMS section U)

- **Built** (design `docs/research/refresh-plans.md`, section 15 says where the build chose): a copy is a load plan and a member of a refresh plan. `rendered/<c>/loads.yml` gains `copy:` on the load plan of a copy with origins on other connections (staging table and the text that creates and drops it, the declared columns, the watermark of an incremental copy, and per origin: connection, engine, table, the read in the origin's dialect with and without the watermark bound, the slice and the inputs as that origin's engine runs them), hashed with the load plan; `Planner.CopyTransfer` builds the transfer for both the deploy and the compiled plan. `connection refresh` runs a copy as, for each origin, transfer, hooks, load, hooks, then one drop of the staging table, under the destination's load lock and in dependency order (the models that read the copied table come after). **The origin check** (the origin is a connection of the project, its read login is in the environment, it answers `SELECT 1` through `ReadSession`) runs before anything is written; a failure is `failed: origin` (new `LoadStatus.FailedOrigin`), `on_failure` applies, and the driver's message is never echoed. The bound of an incremental copy is read from the destination when the copy's turn comes; `source` resolvers of a copy run in a refresh once per origin on that origin's read login (the event's inputs carry the `origin` for a copy of several). The `transfer` rows of `run_log` carry the load's membership; `monitor <plan>` leaves them out of its list of loads. The transfer steps are kept out of the plan the engine opens up front, so one unreachable origin fails one load instead of refusing the refresh. A copy of a native model stays out of a plan (with its reason). `fan_in_fan_out`: `warehouse.orders` is incremental (`unique_key`, `watermark` on `ordered_on`, lookback 7 days) and in `hourly` and `daily`; README, config comments and the template test updated. DESIGN.md 6.6.4, concepts, operations, the agent-kit SKILL.md, `refresh-plans.md` section 15 and OPEN-ITEMS U updated. **No layout 8, no new diagnostic code, no help-text change** (`docs/commands.md` unchanged). `max_parallel` is not built: it limits concurrent sessions at an origin, and there is no concurrency before step 5.
- **Tests**: unit 1977 passed (new `RefreshCopyTests`: the copy part of a load plan from two shops of two engines, membership and order, default plan, damaged copy part; the template test now asserts the copy is a load ahead of the report). Real engines, SQL Server 2022 and PostgreSQL 17 in docker: `Group=apply` 152 passed and 3 skipped (new, in both directions: a copy in `hourly` with a dry run that writes nothing, an incremental read that replaces what it re-reads, the report after the copy, no staging table left, the transfer row with its origin and membership, monitor and status, an origin without its login failing the copy as `failed: origin` and skipping the report, `--only-failed` repeating after the shop is back; a `source` resolver of a copy running in a refresh at the origin with the value recorded as `resolver:source`), `Group=templates` 15 passed (`fan_in_fan_out` now refreshes `hourly` on SQL Server to bring a new order from a shop, the report follows, the other engine waits for its own refresh, and a missing shop login fails the copy and skips the report), the quick group 51 passed and 6 skipped (engines not started: Oracle, Spark, BigQuery, SQL Server 2025), `Group=release` 4 passed.
- **Not verified**: Fabric (never run); the terminal interface and the web page; a fan-in copy with a SQL Server origin, and a `source` resolver on a copy with several origins, in a refresh (the engines' directions are covered with one origin each, and the fan-in template has two PostgreSQL origins); a refresh whose write login lacks rights to create the staging table in the tracking schema (it fails as a load).

## 155. Refresh plans, step 5: parallelism (OPEN-ITEMS section U)

- **Built** (design `docs/research/refresh-plans.md`, section 16 says where the build chose): `parallel: n` on a plan and `--parallel n` on `connection refresh` (flag wins; default 1 is the old one-after-another run). `RefreshScheduler` starts a load when the loads of the run it reads (project graph) and an earlier load of the same model have ended, in plan order among the ready, up to n at once; all decisions run on one coordinator thread. `stop` starts nothing new and lets running loads finish (not cancelled); `continue` skips only dependents; `ignore`, `--only-failed`, `on_busy`, `max_runtime`, `expected_every` unchanged. Each running load has a worker (read session, write session as a `MutationGate` sibling sharing command, permissions, statement log and ordinal, and a tracking session for central tracking). The statement log is line-atomic with ordinals taken together with their opening line. Results are listed in plan order; progress lines are in start order. A dry run goes one load at a time. `connections.<name>.max_parallel` (1 to 64; `config.schema.json`) caps the sessions one run has at a connection: read sessions at an origin (`SessionLimits`), `parallel` itself at the destination. The document gained `parallel` (schema), `docs/commands.md` regenerated (the generator now prints `<number>` for a nullable number).
- **Tests**: unit 1994 passed (new `RefreshParallelTests` 17: the scheduler on its own including a 25-round random stress, the engine with a fake load runner, `SessionLimits`, `max_parallel` parsing). Real engines, SQL Server 2022 and PostgreSQL 17 in docker: `Group=apply` 159 passed and 3 skipped (new, on both engines: `--parallel 3` ends with the same data and states as 1 and the three pausing loads overlap while a dependent chain waits, flag over plan, destination `max_parallel` 1 serialises; a failure under parallel for continue, only-failed and stop; two parallel refreshes lock per load), `Group=templates` 15 passed, the quick group 51 passed and 6 skipped, `Group=release` 4 passed, plus the new origin test (PostgreSQL origin with slow views: 3 sessions uncapped, 1 with `max_parallel: 1`).
- **Decisions for the owner**: `stop` lets running loads finish instead of cancelling them (the design's own words, "loads in flight finish"); a `stop` run is therefore not reproducible load for load; two loads of one model never overlap; a project graph that cannot be read makes the run sequential silently; `max_parallel` does not limit other processes nor the tracking connection; the destination cap counts loads (two sessions each), not sessions; a dry run ignores `--parallel`.
- **Not verified**: Fabric (never run); the terminal interface and the web page for `--parallel`; a SQL Server origin under `max_parallel` (the origin test uses PostgreSQL slow views, because a T-SQL view cannot wait); more than 3 loads at once on the engines; hooks or native models that share a scratch object are not ordered by the graph.

## 156. Refresh plans: the owner's decisions on steps 4 and 5 (OPEN-ITEMS section U)

- **Built** (`docs/research/refresh-plans.md` section 17; `docs/research/refresh-decisions-steps-4-5.md` has a "Decided" line under each item): (A2) an origin of a copy is checked (connection of the project, read login, `SELECT 1`) just before its own transfer, in a refresh (`failed: origin`, the origins before it stay loaded, a `run_log` row under the transfer step so `--only-failed` repeats it) and in a deploy (the up-front probe of every origin and the missing-login refusal are gone; `ApplyOptions.CheckOrigin`); the DDB-230 structure check moved to that point (`plan` reads no origin any more; `on_mismatch: skip` leaves the origin's transfer and load out of the apply). (A3) a copy takes `loads:`; `full_replace` of a copy means everything of the origin and reads it with no watermark bound (a sliced copy replaces the origin's rows by the slice column); the key of a defaulted `delete_insert_by_key` of a sliced copy leads to the slice; `connection deploy --full-refresh` is removed (0.5.0 not cut), the replacement is `--op <copy>=full`, `<copy>.full` or a plan (`weekly: { load: full }`); `fan_in_fan_out` has `default` and `full` and a plan `weekly`. (A5) resolver text is in `loads.yml` in the input's entry (hashed with the plan); the `input.*.sql` files and their code are gone; `resolver_file` left the JSON. (B3) `parallel_effective` in the refresh document and a line plus a warning when `--parallel` is above 1 and the project graph cannot be read. (A1, A4, A2's commit granularity) are items in OPEN-ITEMS U.
- **Tests**: unit 1998 passed (new: copy `loads` with a `full` load sliced and unsliced, the template's `full` read and `weekly` plan, resolver text in `loads.yml` and refused when edited, `ReadGraphFor`). Real engines, SQL Server 2022 and PostgreSQL 17 in docker: `Group=apply` 159 passed and 3 skipped; `Group=templates` 15 passed; the quick group 51 passed and 6 skipped; `Group=release` 4 passed. Conformance rewritten or added: the fan-in copy (skew stops the apply at that origin with the earlier origin loaded, `on_mismatch: skip`, an origin with no read login), the incremental copy's `full` load removing a row deleted at the origin on both directions, `fan_in_fan_out` (east loaded while west is unreachable; `weekly` removes a deleted order).
- **Decisions for the owner**: the plan-time DDB-230 check was removed rather than kept beside the apply-time one (the owner's words: apply it per origin at that point), so `plan` no longer warns about a drifted origin; a `source` resolver is still resolved before the first origin (OPEN-ITEMS U); the `strategy` shown in `loads.yml` for a sliced copy's `full` load is `delete_insert_by_key` (what it runs as).
- **Observed**: a deploy plan's id is the date and a hash of its steps, so two same-day deploys with identical steps get one id and the second is refused (DDB-438); not changed (OPEN-ITEMS U).
- **Not verified**: Fabric (never run); the terminal interface and web page; a write login without `CREATE TABLE` for the staging table; a `--parallel` run whose project graph really cannot be read on an engine (only `ReadGraphFor` is tested, with a failing reader); SQL Server 2025 and the other dialect-only engines were not started.

## 157. Read-only origins, step 1: `engine` keywords, `readonly`, dialect profiles, the row source (OPEN-ITEMS section V)

- **Built** (design `docs/research/read-origins.md`; DESIGN.md 9.4.2 is the as-built text): `engine` is a list of keywords (space- or comma-separated, or a YAML list) in three closed classes, parsed once in the loader into `ConnectionKind(Access, Driver, Dialect)` (`ConnectionKeywords.Parse`; `ConnectionConfig.Kind`; `ConnectionConfig.Engine` stays the driver, so nothing else reads the text). The rules of the design's section 3 are enforced, each an error that names the choices (DDB-106): one driver, at most one access and one dialect, a repeated or unknown keyword, no dialect keyword with `sqlserver`/`postgres`/`fabric`/`duckdb`, `odbc`/`flightsql`/`duckdb` need `readonly`; `odbc` and `flightsql` parse but are refused as "not built yet". `readonly`: `LoginSettings.FromEnvironment(connection, Login.Write)` answers DDB-455 and never reads the environment; DDB-455 is also `tracking.connection` (project's or a connection's), a model's connection (the destination of a build or a copy, `ProjectValidator`), and the connection of `deploy`, `refresh`, `seed`, `publish`, `init`, `ack`, `apply` (`CommandTargets.Resolve`; `inspect`, `status`, `monitor`, `compare` and `project import` pass `readOnlyOk`); `connection inspect` says a readonly connection has no write login. `dialects:` at the project (`schemas/config.schema.json`): `extends`, `quote`, `parameters`, `ping`, `catalog`, `identifier_case`, `limit`, `types`; built in `sqlserver`, `postgres`, `fabric`, `duckdb`, `ansi`; an invalid profile or a reserved name is DDB-456. A profile is a record (`DialectProfile`), not an `ITarget`. `IRowSource`/`IRowReader` under `ReadSession` with the ADO.NET implementation (`AdoRowSource`, in `ReadSession.cs` so `GateInvariantTests` is unchanged); parameters are bound by name or, for a positional dialect, in the order given. `Planner.CopyTransfer` quotes with the origin's profile and rewrites the placeholders of a positional or numbered dialect (`ParameterPlaceholders`), recording `parameter_order` in the plan's transfer (and beside `read_incremental` in `loads.yml`; `schemas/plan.schema.json`); apply binds in that order. For `sqlserver`/`postgres`/`fabric` origins the text, the plans and `loads.yml` are byte-identical (no golden changed). `fan_in_fan_out`'s shops are `readonly postgres`; the metadata document gets `read_only` and `dialect` where they apply. `allow_native_commands` is refused on a connection that is not SQL Server or PostgreSQL.
- **Tests**: unit 2060 passed (new `ReadOriginsTests` 62: the keyword rules in a table, the loader, dialect profiles and every invalid case, quoting identical to the targets' DDL for hostile names, DDB-455 at tracking, model destination, write login and each command, `inspect`, the placeholder rewrite with a name used twice, the copy's read and order for ansi, numbered and a user dialect, a native text with a value used twice, the read session over a fake row source). Real engines, SQL Server 2022 and PostgreSQL 17 in docker: quick group 51 passed and 6 skipped; `Group=templates` 15 passed (the fan-in template with `readonly postgres` shops: the test checks `connection seed` is refused, takes `readonly` out to seed the demo shops, puts it back, and the rest is unchanged); `Group=apply` 159 passed and 3 skipped (unchanged from entry 156, run on this commit); `Group=release` is in entry 158.
- **Decisions for the owner**: (1) a bad `engine` keyword is DDB-106, not a code of its own; DDB-455 is for what `readonly` forbids and DDB-456 for profiles. (2) `readonly` also refuses `ack` and `apply`, not only the five commands the design lists (both write tracking). (3) `connection seed` refuses a readonly connection, so the template's demo shops are seeded with `readonly` taken out for the moment (README step 1). (4) `limit`, `identifier_case` and the `catalog` choice are recorded and validated but nothing reads them yet. (5) `allow_native_commands` is for the engines the tool builds on. (6) `ConnectionConfig.Engine` is still the driver name; the three keywords are in `ConnectionConfig.Kind`.
- **Not verified**: Fabric (never run); the terminal interface and the web page for the new setting; `help` has no page on dialects (the commands' text did not change, `docs/commands.md` is unchanged); a user dialect has no driver to use it until step 3 (its planning is tested, not its use).

## 158. Read-only origins, step 2: a DuckDB file as an origin (OPEN-ITEMS section V)

- **Built** (DESIGN.md 9.4.2): `engine: readonly duckdb`. The login `DBDATABUILD_<NAME>_READ` is the **path of a `.duckdb` file**, opened with `access_mode=READ_ONLY` through DuckDB.NET (already a dependency; now also a reference of `DbDataBuild.Execution`), or `:memory:`. A missing file is an error and is not created; the path is never printed (`DuckDB file, read-only (VARIABLE)` is the login in a header). `DuckDbRowSource` (in `ReadSession.cs`) is the ADO.NET source with positional binding and a `LIMIT 0` describe. Works for: a **copy** (full, incremental with the watermark as `?` and `parameter_order`, fan-in over files, in a deploy and in a refresh, including `--only-failed` after a missing file is `failed: origin`); a **mapped model** (`project import` and `--check` read `information_schema` and `duckdb_constraints()`: tables, views, columns, primary and foreign keys; `SourceTypes` has the DuckDB map, with what has no logical type said); the **structure check** DDB-230 at apply; a **`source` resolver** (the lowered DuckDB text is the origin's text, validated by DuckDB's parser, not transpiled); a **native query**, including one over a Parquet or CSV file on a `:memory:` origin (the file name is a bound parameter: `read_parquet(?)`), shape-checked with its values; `connection inspect` (the dialect's ping, DuckDB's version); `status`, `monitor`. `connection compare` refuses a connection that is not SQL Server or PostgreSQL; `track_definition` finds no routine on a DuckDB file; `BLOB` read as a stream is turned into bytes. A refresh pings an origin with its dialect's ping.
- **Tests**: unit 2079 passed (new: `DuckDbOriginTests` 8, `DuckDbOriginProjectTests` 9 on a real file and `:memory:`: read-only open and no creation, the layer under the guard refusing a write, Parquet and CSV, positional binding with a value used twice, every logical type converted, concurrent sessions on one file, the catalog and types, inspect, import and check, compare refused, a model refused on a duckdb connection, the plan and `loads.yml` of a copy, the DDB-230 check, a native query planned, shape-checked and read, the resolver text; `ReadOriginsTests` gained the native-command and driver cases). Real engines, SQL Server 2022 and PostgreSQL 17 in docker, on the final code of both steps: `Group=apply` 167 passed and 3 skipped (8 new, `DuckDbOriginConformanceTests`, each to SQL Server and PostgreSQL: every type, NULLs, unicode and a multi-line text intact against DuckDB's own text of the rows, a dry run, a second deploy; an incremental refresh with the dependent model after it, `--dry-run`, `failed: origin` for a missing file without creating it, then `--only-failed`; a `source` resolver whose value is in the plan; a native query over a Parquet file on a `:memory:` origin); `Group=templates` 15 passed; the quick group 51 passed and 6 skipped; `Group=release` 4 passed.
- **Decisions for the owner**: (1) a `:memory:` origin lets the project's SQL read any file the tool's process can read (`read_parquet`, `read_text`, `glob`): the sandbox the `expression` resolvers have (external access off) does not apply to an origin, because reading files is what it is for; the guard still allows SELECT only. (2) DuckDB does not say which schema a foreign key refers to; `project import` reads it as the same schema. (3) Indexes of a DuckDB file are not read (an ART index is rare; its definition is a `CREATE INDEX` text). (4) `connection compare` and native commands are not offered for it. (5) The read login's value is a path, so a relative path means relative to the directory the tool runs in.
- **Not verified**: Fabric (never run); the terminal interface and the web page for a DuckDB connection; Windows paths and file locking (the file read-only while another process writes it); a DuckDB file on a network share; a very large file (the 300,000-row bulk test is for the engines); `max_parallel` on a file origin beyond two concurrent sessions; a file written by a DuckDB version other than 1.5 (the library's own format check decides).


## 159. Read-only origins, step 3: an ODBC source as an origin (OPEN-ITEMS section V)

- **Built** (DESIGN.md 9.4.2; design `docs/research/read-origins.md`, section 8a): `engine: readonly odbc [dialect]` (`ConnectionKeywords.IsBuilt` now lets `odbc` through; `flightsql` is still refused). The login `DBDATABUILD_<NAME>_READ` is an ODBC connection string or a DSN with its credentials (a bare name is `DSN=name`; `OdbcLogin`), handed to the driver manager as written and never printed (the header shows the variable and `UID`). `OdbcRowSource` (in `ReadSession.cs`) over `System.Data.Odbc` 10.0.13: positional `?` values bound in the plan's `parameter_order` (a name used twice is passed twice), ADO types mapped to ODBC's, command timeout through the provider, login timeout 30 s, streaming reads, a native text described with the driver's schema-only mode. The catalog is read through `information_schema` (`CatalogReader`, `SourceCatalogReader`: columns, primary and foreign keys; no indexes) with the profile's `types:` (`SourceTypes.From("odbc", column, profile)`); `catalog: none` is said plainly by `project import` and `connection inspect` and skips the copy's structure check with a note. `ConnectionConfig.Profile` carries a project's own dialect to every login (step 1 had no driver to use it). `DriverFailure` names a missing driver manager and a missing or unloadable driver (SQLSTATEs `IM002`, `IM003`, unixODBC's 01000 "Can't open lib", `IM004`, `IM010`, `IM012`, `IM014`) and otherwise prints the type and the SQLSTATE; `connection inspect`, the origin check of a copy and a refresh (`failed: origin`), a session that cannot be opened and the resolvers use it. `TransferValues` reads a boolean handed over as text (psqlodbc's default). A `source` resolver is sent as the lowered text (non-engine origins share `LoadRenderer`'s DuckDB-validated path). Docs: operations.md (the ODBC source and an "ODBC setup" note for Linux, macOS and Windows), concepts, SKILL.md, config schema text. Test setup: `scripts/fetch-psqlodbc.sh` (psqlodbc, libpq and libodbcinst unpacked without root) and `scripts/test-engines.sh env` exports `DBDATABUILD_TEST_ODBC_PG_DRIVER` and `LD_LIBRARY_PATH` when a driver is there; the tests skip with the reason when it is not.
- **Tests**: unit 2110 passed (new `OdbcOriginTests` 31: the login value and its user, the described login never holds the value, a project dialect reaching every way of asking for a login, binding order and ODBC types, the catalog queries on a recording source with values by position and `catalog: none`, import saying so, the types of a profile, text booleans, the missing-manager and missing-driver sentences, inspect quoting nothing). Real engines (SQL Server 2022 and PostgreSQL 17 in docker; origin PostgreSQL 17 through psqlodbc 16 and unixODBC 2.3.12): `Group=apply` 179 passed and 3 skipped (12 new, `OdbcOriginConformanceTests`, each copy to SQL Server and PostgreSQL: every type, NULLs, unicode intact against the origin's own text and a second deploy; an incremental refresh with the positional watermark, `failed: origin` for a driver that is not installed with nothing of the login in the output, then `--only-failed`; a `source` resolver; a native query with a value used twice and a DDB-230 for a declaration that disagrees; `project import` of keys and types and DDB-230 for a changed origin column; plus `max_parallel` capping a refresh at one session and a 1 s timeout stopping a statement); `Group=templates` 15 passed; the quick group 51 passed and 6 skipped; `Group=release` 4 passed.
- **Decisions for the owner**: (1) a driver whose boolean arrives as text is read (`t`/`f`...), instead of requiring `BoolsAsChar=0`. (2) `catalog: none` makes `project import` exit with findings and the structure check a note, never a guess. (3) A `source` resolver at an ODBC origin is the lowered DuckDB text, validated by DuckDB's parser; the author writes SQL both accept. (4) `ConnectionConfig.Profile` was added so a user dialect works; the string-named `FromEnvironment` overloads of the engines are unchanged. (5) `DriverFailure` reads unixODBC's warning text only to classify it and never repeats it. (6) The schemas `information_schema`, `pg_catalog` and `sys` are left out of the ODBC schema list.
- **Not verified**: any driver but psqlodbc; Windows and macOS driver managers (the setup text for them is from knowledge, not tried); SQL Server through msodbcsql; a driver that ignores timeouts; a system with no `information_schema` (only the `catalog: none` path); the terminal interface and web page for an ODBC connection; Fabric (never run). The missing-driver-manager message is tested by exception mapping, not by a machine without libodbc.

## 160. Read-only origins, step 4: an Arrow Flight SQL server as an origin (OPEN-ITEMS section V)

- **Built** (DESIGN.md 9.4.2; design `docs/research/read-origins.md`, section 8b): `engine: readonly flightsql [dialect]`. Client `Apache.Arrow.Flight.Sql` 23.0.0 (managed; chosen over the ADBC Flight SQL driver, a wrapper over the same Flight library; no native binary, so the single-file publish is unchanged; no NuGet advisory, `Pkcs` pins untouched). The login is a URI with `;` options (`FlightSqlLogin`: `token`, `token_env`, `token_command`, `user`+`password` through the basic handshake, `tls_skip_verify`, `ca_file`, `header.<name>`); no OIDC flow, a token from outside the tool (`token_command` is started without a shell). `FlightSqlRowSource` (own file; `GateInvariantTests` pins the client to it): plain query without parameters, prepared statement with typed Arrow parameters in `parameter_order`, rows read from record batches value by value (`ArrowConversions`), the command timeout as a real gRPC deadline by interceptor (the library ignores `FlightCallOptions.Timeout`), the prepared exchange made from the protocol messages (the library's class refuses empty schemas). Catalog by the metadata calls (built from the messages: the library sends `""` filters) then `information_schema`; `CatalogUnavailableException` says plainly when there is neither. Failures by gRPC status in `DriverFailure.FlightSql`; `failed: origin` for a copy; nothing of the value or the server's text is printed (header: `host:port` and the kind of sign-in). Docs: operations.md ("Flight SQL setup" with the auth options), concepts, SKILL.md, config schema, THIRD-PARTY-NOTICES. Test setup: `scripts/test-engines.sh up flight` starts two voltrondata/sqlflite containers (plain and TLS) and `env` exports `DBDATABUILD_TEST_FLIGHT`, `_TLS`, `_CA`; the conformance project hosts an in-process Flight SQL server (DuckDB, typed parameters) so most tests need no container.
- **Tests**: unit 2153 passed (new `FlightSqlOriginTests` 42: the login grammar and its refusals, descriptions without the value, token sources and the command, failure sentences by gRPC status including the wrapped ones, every Arrow value and type, the parameter batch in order, the catalog by metadata, by information_schema and with neither; a gate scan for the client). Real engines (SQL Server 2022, PostgreSQL 17, sqlflite plain and TLS in docker): `Group=apply` 199 passed and 3 skipped (12 new `FlightSqlOriginConformanceTests` with the in-process server and 8 new `FlightSqlServerConformanceTests` with sqlflite: rows intact into both engines by user and password, token, token_env and token_command, TLS with ca_file and skip, wrong name and untrusted certificate; typed incremental refresh and `failed: origin` for an unreachable server and a refused token; `source` resolver; native query with a value used three times; `project import` by metadata calls and by information_schema; `max_parallel`; the deadline); `Group=templates` 15 passed; the quick group 51 passed and 6 skipped; `Group=release` 4 passed.
- **Decisions for the owner**: (1) `Apache.Arrow.Flight.Sql` rather than the ADBC driver. (2) Entra ID/OIDC only as a token obtained outside (`token_command`), not a login flow. (3) Metadata calls first, `information_schema` second; the structure check does not compare nullability on Flight SQL origins. (4) A `token_command` runs a program named in the environment value, without a shell. (5) The default call deadline is 30 s (none for a copy's read).
- **Not verified**: the owner's proxy; Entra ID/OIDC tokens; Windows (`token_command`, TLS trust store); mTLS; a server with several endpoints or locations; servers other than sqlflite and the in-process one; a token that expires during a copy; Fabric (never run).

## 161. Read-only origins, step 5: the catalog on the new origins (OPEN-ITEMS section V)

- **Built** (DESIGN.md 9.4.2, "The catalog on the new origins"; research doc sections 8c and 10): (1) `catalog: odbc`, a third choice beside `information_schema` and `none` (profile grammar, `schemas/config.schema.json`, DDB-456 text; DDB-456 also when a non-`odbc` connection uses it): the driver's own catalog functions `SQLTables`, `SQLColumns`, `SQLPrimaryKeys`, `SQLForeignKeys`, `SQLStatistics`, called through the driver manager (`OdbcCatalogClient.cs`: `OdbcNativeFunctions` on a connection of its own, `OdbcCatalog` pure over the rows the ODBC specification fixes) because `System.Data.Odbc` offers no keys; it executes no statement (a test scans the file for any executing call). `OdbcRowSource` is an `IMetadataCatalog` now (as Flight SQL's metadata calls are); `project import`, the DDB-230 check and `connection inspect` use it; a driver that lacks a function is said to lack it (keys and indexes become a note, tables and columns a `CatalogUnavailableException` naming the function). Types: the driver's `TYPE_NAME` when the dialect's `types:` has it, else the standard name of its ODBC type code. (2) Indexes: `SourceCatalogReader.IndexesAsync` (DuckDB `duckdb_indexes()`, the key columns read from the `CREATE INDEX` text; ODBC `SQLStatistics`; none for Flight SQL, `information_schema` or the engines, which carry theirs); `project import` merges them into the shape and says in words where there are none (`catalog_notes` in the text and the JSON document, a new key of `data_project_import`). (3) DuckDB foreign keys report the schema in the constraint text; DuckDB 1.5 refuses foreign keys across schemas, so today it is always the key's own schema (a conformance test pins that). (4) `limit` writes the zero-row describe read of a native query (`DialectProfile.Bounded`: ODBC when schema-only fails or says nothing, Flight SQL when the server gives no schema, DuckDB as before, the same text); `identifier_case` is used by the structure check (`CatalogReader.FindAsync`, `DialectProfile.Fold`: a declared table found only in the folded case is a DDB-230 difference that says how it is stored); neither changes a copy's read (a fold there would alter `readonly odbc postgres`); there is no sample, preview or row count of an origin, so no other bounded read exists. (5) `dbdatabuild help dialects` (CommandSpecs entry, `data_help_dialects` in the output schema, docs/commands.md, the TUI catalog, SKILL.md, concepts, agents permissions): the fields, the built-ins (generated from the data) and an example. **A fix found on the way:** a built-in engine dialect with `odbc` or `flightsql` (`readonly flightsql postgres`), which the keyword rules accepted, bound `@name` and had no type names; `DialectProfiles.ForDriver` (applied by the loader to `ConnectionConfig.Profile`) makes those drivers read it by position, and the engine profiles carry the standard type names plus their own. The earlier example profile named after a proxy is gone from the code, tests, schema and docs: the dialect is the one of the system behind a connection and a proxy is several connections (`header.x-datasource=<name>` in each login). The owner's proxy source was read with `gh` (read only): see research doc 8c for what it offers; step 6 (the dialect from the server through `GetSqlInfo`, recorded in a lock file) is designed in section 10 and OPEN-ITEMS V and not built.
- **Tests**: unit 2189; conformance on SQL Server 2022 and PostgreSQL 17: `apply` 203 passed (3 skipped), `templates` 15, quick 61 (6 skipped), `release` 4
- **Decisions for the owner**: (1) the native ODBC catalog client (P/Invoke of the driver manager) instead of `GetSchema`, which has no keys; it opens a second connection with the same login. (2) ODBC indexes are read from `SQLStatistics` for every ODBC source, not only `catalog: odbc`, and a failure is a note. (3) `identifier_case` only informs the structure check; the read stays as declared. (4) A built-in engine dialect takes `?` and the engine's type names on `odbc` and `flightsql`. (5) Step 6: lock file (`dialects.lock.yml` written by `connection inspect --adopt-dialect`) or plan-only; the proxy's custom `GetSqlInfo` id. (6) The proxy's ODBC sources report no primary keys; asking it to answer `GetImportedKeys` and primary keys for ODBC sources would let the tool import grains and keys through it.
- **Not verified**: a driver other than psqlodbc (so `catalog: odbc` with a text, Excel or Access driver, or with SQL Server's msodbcsql, is untried; the type codes and the `SQLStatistics` rows of other drivers may differ from the specification's); Windows and macOS driver managers (the library names are the provider's, not tried); a running proxy; the zero-row fallback of ODBC and Flight SQL against a real driver that refuses schema-only (the Flight SQL fallback ran against the in-process server and the text of all three styles on SQL Server and PostgreSQL, the ODBC fallback path itself only by reading); the terminal interface and web page for these origins; Fabric (never run).

## 162. `project update`: the dialect from the server and the metadata from the connections (OPEN-ITEMS section V, step 6)

- **Built** (DESIGN.md 9.4.3; research doc 10a): (1) `dbdatabuild project update [selectors] [--offline] [--check] [--answers] [--accept-inferred]`, a project-wide command (CommandSpecs, `C->P`, `data_project_update` in the output schema, docs/commands.md, the TUI catalog with a confirmation unless `--check`, MCP like the other commands, SKILL.md): the definitions of the models (`DefineCommand`, non-interactive), the dialects of the connections and the metadata of the mapped models. (2) **Dialects**: a `readonly flightsql` connection with no dialect keyword (`ConnectionKind.DialectDeclared` is the new bit) is asked `GetSqlInfo` (`FlightSqlRowSource.ServerFactsAsync`, `ReadSession.ServerFactsAsync`, ids 0, 1, 3, 503, 504, 505), `DialectDiscovery.Choose` maps the answer (product name to a built-in or a project dialect, else `ansi`; the reported quote and an upper identifier case correct the profile), and the result is committed in `dialects.lock.yml` (`DialectLockFile`: format 1, per connection the reported facts, the dialect, the profile fields and `profile_hash`; deterministic; hand edits are caught by the hash, DDB-457). `ProjectConfigLoader.LoadFromProject` lays the entries over the connections (`ConnectionConfig.Profile`, `.DialectLock`), so compile, the planner and the logins use them as they use a keyword; an explicit keyword wins (DDB-459 for an unused entry). Plans (`transfer.dialect`, `dialect_hash`) and `loads.yml` (the same beside `parameter_order`) carry the name and hash for Flight SQL origins only, so every other rendered file is byte for byte as before. The origin check (`OriginAccess.CheckAsync`: a deploy's apply and a refresh) asks a connection with a committed dialect again and fails the origin (DDB-460) naming the difference. A compile, `validate` or plan of a project with a copy reading from a Flight SQL connection that has no entry warns (DDB-458) and writes for `ansi`. ODBC, DuckDB and the built-in engines have nothing to discover; the command says so. (3) **Metadata**: `ConnectionCapture` (built on `ImportSourcesCommand.GatherAsync`, split out of `project import`, which behaves as before) compares every mapped model in scope, copy origins included, with its table on the first connection of its `connections` that has a read login and answers (columns, types, nullability, primary key as grain, foreign keys, indexes, as `project import` writes them); a native model is said to be skipped; a connection with no read login or unreachable is skipped with a plain note and the exit code is untouched. (4) **`project model update`** now has the same online part, `--offline` and `--check` (its effect class is target read-only, `C->P`; a path naming a mapped model is accepted); the online default is the one constant `UpdateDefaults.OnlineByDefault`. (5) `--check` writes nothing, shows a diff per file and exits 1 with DDB-461 per difference; a server version alone is no difference. (6) The in-process Flight SQL server of the conformance tests answers `GetSqlInfo` (`SayAboutItself`). New codes DDB-457 to DDB-461.
- **The proxy** (read with `gh` on 2026-10-14, HEAD of main): per data source it sends the server name (`Microsoft SQL Server`, `DuckDB`, or the ODBC driver's DBMS name), version, read-only, SQL, Substrait, cancel and bulk-ingestion flags, and `SQL_IDENTIFIER_QUOTE_CHAR`, `SQL_IDENTIFIER_CASE`, `SQL_QUOTED_IDENTIFIER_CASE` (case-insensitive) for SQL Server and DuckDB sources. It sends **no dialect name and no parameter style** (the owner's note says parameter style is sent; the code at HEAD does not, and the protocol has no id for it); the tool therefore picks the dialect from the product name and always binds a Flight SQL connection by position.
- **Tests**: unit 2231 passed (new: `DialectDiscoveryTests` 14 for the mapping, the hash, the lock file and its refusals; `ProjectUpdateDialectTests` 13 with a fake server for new/changed/unchanged/version-only/skipped/offline/explicit-wins, compile and plan carrying the dialect, the origin check; `ProjectUpdateSourcesTests` 7 on a DuckDB file for both commands; the lock-file fuzz in `FuzzTests`); conformance quick group 61 passed 6 skipped, apply 199 passed 11 skipped (new `ProjectUpdateConformanceTests`: a DuckDB file, psqlodbc, the in-process Flight SQL server answering GetSqlInfo with a changed dialect failing the origin of a deploy on PostgreSQL), templates and release 15 passed. Oracle, Spark, BigQuery and SQL Server 2025 engines were not started.
- **Decisions for the owner**: (1) The file is `dialects.lock.yml` at the project root (a lock file, not plan-only); the server version is recorded but is not a difference. (2) A product name the tool does not know is `ansi` with the reported quote, not an error; the person adds a profile named after the product (`clickhouse`) or names the dialect in `engine`. (3) `project update` writes by default and `--check` is the dry form (as `project compile`), so there is no `--write`; the online default is `UpdateDefaults.OnlineByDefault` for V-F1. (4) `project model update` is now a connection-reading command (effect class and header changed, path of a mapped model accepted); the DefineCommand header still says `connection: none`. (5) A mapped model on several connections (fan-in) is compared with the first reachable one only; differences between the stores are not looked for. (6) A server that later says nothing is a changed dialect (`ansi` against the committed one), which fails the origin. (7) The proxy should send its own dialect name (a custom `GetSqlInfo` id) if the product name is not enough, and `GetImportedKeys`; the contract is in research doc section 10.
- **Not verified**: a running proxy (the ids come from reading its source); a Flight SQL server other than the in-process one answering GetSqlInfo; the dialect check at a refresh against a real engine destination beyond the PostgreSQL deploy test; ODBC other than psqlodbc; Windows and macOS; the TUI and web page for these commands; Fabric (never run).

## 163. Model kind: one word, one structure (OPEN-ITEMS section V; decision `docs/research/model-kind-structure.md`)

- **Built** (DESIGN.md 6.1 "How a kind is written", 9.4.1; `docs/models.md`): the word is "model kind" (a column's SQL type is the only "type" left). `kind` is keyed by the kind's name with the kind's settings beneath it, exactly one key (`kind: {copy: {from: pos.orders}}`, block style the same), or a plain value for a kind with no settings (`kind: view`). `ModelKindShape` reads the node; a malformed one (two kinds, an empty mapping, a list, settings that are not a mapping, an empty value) is the new DDB-248; a setting of another kind is an unknown key (DDB-104) because each kind's settings are a closed set; `kind.copy:` is an ordinary key with a dot and is not supported. `ModelDefinitionLoader` and `SourceDescriptorLoader` (mapped, native, and `kind: native` with the text in a `.native.sql`) read it; `schemas/model.schema.json` is a one-of per kind with closed keys and the `if`/`then` conditions test the new shapes, `source.schema.json` takes `mapped` and `native`. `kind` is replaced whole by the nearer layer (`YamlMerge`), never merged key by key; the query head builds its layer in the new form. `ModelDefinition.KindType` is now `Kind` (and `QueryHead.Kind`).
- **The old form** `kind: {type: copy, ...}` still loads for this release as the same kind with the same settings; the rendered files are byte-identical (`ModelKindShapeTests` compiles one project in both forms and compares every file). DDB-247 (warning) says so once for each file that writes it (the model's own, or the folder or project file that gave the kind). `project model update` rewrites it (`KindFormRewriter`: text splices located by the parser's offsets, so comments, key order, CRLF and block scalars survive; the result is read again and must name the same kind with the same settings or nothing is written) for the model files, mapped, native and copy files, `_dbdatabuild.yml` and `dbdatabuild.yml` (under `defaults:`); `project update` does the same through it; `--check` lists each file as DDB-247 and counts it as a difference; `data.old_kind_forms` in the JSON (schema updated). `model create`, `define`, the head and `project import` write only the new form. Every template, fixture, golden, the agent-kit skill, the docs and `docs/commands.md` were converted by a script that handled flow and block forms, comments, CRLF, and the interpolated and escaped C# strings; the cases it could not (a kind block continued after a concatenation) were found by the tests and fixed by hand.
- **Tests**: unit 2279 passed (new `ModelKindShapeTests` 37: every new form and the shorthand, the refusals, the old form loading, the warning once per file, byte-identical rendering, the folder's kind replaced whole, the rewriter on twenty shapes, `--check`/`--write` through the command and `project update --check`; a fuzz test for the new shape and for the rewriter never producing a file that does not load; schema fixtures for the new refusals; DDB-247/248 in the catalog tests; the merge table in `YamlMergeTests` rewritten for the one exception). Conformance on SQL Server 2022 and PostgreSQL 17: `apply` 199 passed (after fixing one fixture, `CopyConformanceTests`, whose second part of a `kind` was built by concatenation), `templates` 15, quick 61 (6 skipped), `release` 4; with `scripts/test-engines.sh up flight` the Flight SQL tests run too (20 passed). **Why `apply` showed 11 skipped against 3 before:** 8 of them are `FlightSqlServerConformanceTests`, added with the Flight SQL step, which skip unless the Flight servers are up (`up flight`); the other 3 are the two two-million-row timing tests and the SQL Server 2025 test (`up all`), as before. They are not skipped for a fault.
- **Decisions for the owner**: (1) Both `kind: {full: {}}` and `kind: full` are accepted, and an empty value (`kind:\n  full:`) means no settings. (2) The editor schemas refuse the old form (the tool still reads it). (3) The two new codes are DDB-247 (warning, old form) and DDB-248 (error, malformed kind). (4) The definition hash of a mapped model, which is taken over the canonical text the writer produces, changes once because that text now says `kind: mapped` (the metadata documents change anyway in entry 164).
- **Not verified**: the old form read from a folder file together with a new-form model on a real engine beyond the templates; the rewriter on a file with a comment on the `kind:` line itself (it declines and says so); Fabric (never run).

## 164. Model kind in the metadata: a plain string, a stored column, and the dependencies as rows (tracking layout 8; OPEN-ITEMS section V)

- **Found first**: the dependency tree was stored nowhere. A model document carried its direct `upstream`; `metadata_ancestors` computed the closure in the in-memory DuckDB from those; `project show graph` and the selectors computed it from the project files. The model document's `kind` was an object (`{type, unique_key, time_column, lookback}`) whose `type` enum in `schemas/metadata.schema.json` listed four values, so a copy's document was already outside the schema (drift: `copy` was missing; `mapped` and `native` have source documents, which have no kind).
- **Built** (DESIGN.md 9.8, 12 "Layout 8"): (1) The model document is `dbdatabuild.model/2`: `kind` is a plain string and the settings are `kind_settings` (an empty object for `view` and `full`; `unique_key` for `incremental_by_unique_key`; `time_column`, `lookback` for `incremental_by_time_range`; `from`, `slice`, `on_mismatch`, `unique_key`, `watermark` for `copy`); the schema's enum has the five kinds that have a model document and the settings are a closed object. The same documents are what `project compile`, `project show metadata`, MCP and the web page show; the terminal interface and the web page read the new properties. (2) `metadata_document` (so the view `metadata_current`) has `model_kind`, null for a project, a source or a plan; `publish` writes it; the existing `kind` column stays the subject kind. (3) A table `metadata_dependency(connection, subject, depends_on, depends_on_kind, depth)`, key `(connection, subject, depends_on)`: depth 1 is a direct edge (tables read, macros called, a native model's `reads`), a greater depth the shortest path through others; kinds `model`, `source`, `native`, `macro`, `unknown`. It is computed from the project (`MetadataBuilder.Dependencies`, the same reading as a document's `upstream`, so a partial publish still gives each model its whole ancestry) and written by `publish` and `metadata.store_on_apply` beside `object_info`; it is compared with the stored rows and only the subjects that differ are rewritten (so an interrupted publish is finished by the next, and an unchanged project writes nothing); `publish` reports `data.dependencies`. (4) The in-memory views of the metadata rules read the same rows (`metadata_dependency` is a table there; `metadata_upstream` is depth 1, `metadata_ancestors` all of it) and `metadata_models` has `kind` (replacing `kind_type`) and `kind_settings`; the four templates' rules were changed to `kind`. (5) Layout 8: `init` creates the table and alters nothing (on a layout 7 store it keeps the view it has, without `model_kind`, and does not record 8); `init --upgrade --apply` adds the column and records 8; documents stored earlier keep a null `model_kind` until the next publish writes them again.
- **Tests**: unit 2284 passed (new `MetadataDependencyTests`: the closure, the project's rows with their kinds, the document's kind and settings, the in-memory views reading the same rows, which subjects a sync writes, the layout-8 scripts; JSON, schema, DDL and init-count tests moved to the new layout). Conformance on SQL Server 2022 and PostgreSQL 17 (with the Flight SQL servers up): `apply` 209 passed, 3 skipped (the two two-million-row timing tests and the SQL Server 2025 test, which need `up all`), `templates` 15, quick 61 (6 skipped, as before), `release` 4, none failed; new `A_store_of_layout_seven_gets_the_model_kind_column...` runs the 7 to 8 migration on both engines (refused before; `init` keeps the old view and the version; the upgrade; a publish writes the kinds and the rows; a second publish writes nothing), and the three older upgrade tests now end at the current layout.
- **Decisions for the owner**: (1) `metadata_models.kind_type` is renamed `kind` with no alias; a user's own metadata rule that names `kind_type` fails with DuckDB's message (the four templates' rules were changed). (2) A mapped and a native model have a source document with no kind, so `model_kind` is null for them as decided; `metadata_dependency` does include a native model's `reads` (the native model is a subject). If `model_kind` should say `mapped` and `native` for source subjects, the source document would need a kind (a `dbdatabuild.source/2`). (3) One row per (subject, depends_on) with the shortest depth, not one per path. (4) Dependencies are written by a publish of any models for the models published (and the native models they depend on); a full publish also removes the rows of subjects the project no longer has.
- **Not verified**: Fabric (never run; the view guard and the new table are written for it like the rest); the old documents' `model_kind` backfill (not done, by design); a tracking store shared by many connections beyond the one-connection tests.

## 165. Mapped and native models carry a kind in the metadata (`dbdatabuild.source/2`); review of the mapped/native split (OPEN-ITEMS section V)

- **Found first**: `model_kind` was null for a mapped and a native model, and a native model had no stored document at all: `MetadataPublisher.Collect` walked `Project.Descriptors` (mapped only) while the native models were a separate accessor, so they were subjects of `metadata_dependency` with no `metadata_current` row. Dependency kinds said `source` for a mapped model. The review of why the two kinds are `SourceDescriptor` / `dbdatabuild.source` is `docs/research/mapped-native-vs-models.md` (history, what the split costs in fifteen places, options, recommendation: keep two types, one surface, no merge).
- **Built**: (1) The source document is `dbdatabuild.source/2`: `kind` (`mapped` or `native`, `SourceDescriptor.Kind`) and `kind_settings` (empty for a mapped model; `access`, `connections`, `text_hash`, `reads` for a native one, which replaces the `native` object of version 1). The project document's `sources[]` entries also get `kind`. (2) `publish` writes `model_kind` for source documents (`MetadataDependencies.ModelKindOf`, which also reads a version 1 document by its `native` object) and now publishes native models (`FileDescriptors`). No table changed, so the layout stays 8. (3) `depends_on_kind` / `upstream_kind` / the model document's `upstream[].kind` say `mapped` where they said `source` (`model` for the five built kinds, `native`, `macro`, `unknown` as before); the in-memory views agree (`FromDocuments`), `metadata_sources` has `kind` and `kind_settings`, `project show graph` has a `model_kind` for mapped and native nodes (its node `kind` is still `source`: owner decision). (4) The nine templates' rules say `'mapped'`; web page badge shows the kind.
- **For a user's metadata rules**: `upstream_kind = 'source'` is now `'mapped'`; `metadata_current.model_kind` is non-null for model and source documents; `metadata_current.kind` is still the subject kind (`model`/`source`); a document read by a tool has `kind_settings` where a source's `native` was. Documents stored before this keep the old text and a null `model_kind` until the next `publish` (their text changed, so it rewrites all of them; native models were never stored, so they appear on the first publish; a subject the project no longer has keeps its old row). Dependency rows with `source` are rewritten by the next publish (the comparison includes the kind).
- **Tests**: unit 2284 passed (documents, `model_kind` for all four sorts including a version 1 source document, `metadata_sources`, dependency kinds, schema validation with `source/3` refused). Conformance on SQL Server 2022 and PostgreSQL 17 (Flight SQL servers up): `templates` 15, quick 61 (6 skipped), `apply` 209 (3 skipped: two timing tests and SQL Server 2025); the layout 7 to 8 test now expects `src.t|mapped` in `model_kind` and `mapped` dependency rows. `release` group not run (nothing it covers changed).
- **Not verified**: Fabric; a store with rows published by an earlier layout 8 build (only the layout 7 migration test); `release` group.
- **Decisions for the owner**: see the end of the review document (one subject kind `model` with layout 9; a common projection and one selector grammar; graph node kind `source`; `depends_on_kind` as the model kind; merge not recommended).


## 166. The dependency graph is compiled into `loads.yml`; a refresh orders, skips and selects by it and `publish` writes the same rows (OPEN-ITEMS section U)

- **Decided by the owner (2026-10-14)**: the dependency tree is stored whenever the load plan details are computed and stored (compile), so the compiled plan and its graph cannot differ from each other or from the source at compile time.
- **Built** (DESIGN.md 10.6 `loads.yml`, refresh *Parallelism*; `docs/research/refresh-plans.md` sections 6 and 16): (1) `rendered/<c>/loads.yml` has `dependencies: N` in its header and a `dependencies:` list after the loads: one row per (model, depends_on) between two models the file loads, with `model_kind`, `depends_on_kind` (the model kinds), `depth` (1 direct, more the shortest path through anything, a view or a model not loaded on the connection included) in the order subject, depth, name; no timestamps or machine names. It is hashed with the file, so an edited, removed or recounted graph is refused like an edited plan. (2) The rows come from `MetadataBuilder.Dependencies`, called once per compile for all connections and filtered by `MetadataBuilder.LoadDependencies`; `publish` and `metadata.store_on_apply` call the same function (`MetadataPublisher.CollectDependencies`), so there is no second computation. (3) Refresh plans do not carry a graph: they name load plans of the same file by hash and the refresh reads the graph from that file (one place, nothing to disagree). Deploy plans do not carry it. (4) `connection refresh` builds a `LoadGraph` from the file and uses it for what a load waits for, for skipping what reads a failed or skipped load (`DownstreamOf`, which includes `--only-failed` since it reruns through the same engine) and for selectors that the graph can answer: a loaded model's name with `+`, `n+`, `+n`, `@`, `kind:` and `all`/`*`, and `exclude:` of those. Any other selector term (`tag:`, `property:`, `path:`, `connection:`, `changed:`, a file or directory, a model that is not loaded) needs the project's settings or git, so the project is loaded for that selection exactly as before; that is the one remaining project read. (5) A loads file without the graph (compiled before) still parses; its refresh runs one load at a time with `parallel: 1 (the loads file has no dependency graph, ...)`, a `warnings` entry and `parallel_effective` 1, and failure skipping reads the project lazily as it did. `RefreshCommand.ReadGraphFor` is replaced by `ParallelFor`. There is no format version number in `loads.yml`; the `dependencies` header is the marker. (6) `MetadataBuilder.Dependencies` now reads every query variant of a model (the tables its parameters name on other connections), as the dependency graph always did, so `metadata_dependency` can now have edges that the default connection's reading did not.
- **Tests**: unit 2296 passed (new `LoadGraphTests`: the carried edges through an unloaded view with kinds and depth, deterministic and rewritten when the project changes, edit/removal/recount/depth 0/unknown model/renamed key refused, an old file reads with no graph, walks and selectors by depth, selection with the models folder deleted after compile, 300 seeded mutations of `loads.yml` never throw, and for each of the six sample projects the loads graph equals the published rows filtered to the loaded models; `RefreshParallelTests` ParallelFor). Conformance on SQL Server 2022 and PostgreSQL 17 (Flight SQL servers up): `apply` 209 passed, 3 skipped (as before), `templates` 15, quick 61 (6 skipped, as before), none failed; the parallel test now also runs `--parallel 3` with the models folder moved away (effective 3, same result) and with a graph-less `loads.yml` (effective 1 and the warning). No golden file contains `loads.yml`, so none changed.
- **Decisions for the owner**: (1) The graph holds only edges between loaded models, with the kinds being model kinds; edges to sources, macros and unloaded models are in `metadata_dependency` but not in `loads.yml`. (2) Refresh plans derive from the file's graph rather than carry their own. (3) Per-connection variants: the graph is the union over a model's variants, not the variant of this connection; an edge that exists only on another connection can make a parallel run wait a little more than needed, never less. (4) A selection with a term the graph cannot answer still loads the project (the alternative, refusing those selectors in a refresh, was not taken). (5) `project compile` analyses every model once more for the rows (the cost of the one function). (6) `project show refresh` and its JSON do not show the graph yet.
- **Not verified**: Fabric; a graph-bearing `loads.yml` compiled by this version run by an older tool (it would refuse the unknown header key by design); the effect on very large projects' compile time.

## 167. One model surface: mapped and native models are models everywhere (owner direction 2026-10-14: consistency, not compatibility; the review's option (a), stage 2; OPEN-ITEMS section V)

- **Why**: the review `docs/research/mapped-native-vs-models.md` listed fifteen places where a tool or person saw a mapped or native model as something other than a model. The owner said to carry stage 2 through, break names freely and say what broke. The two C# classes stay (the merge is not smaller than the review estimated: the renderer, lowerer, planner and refresh planner assume a query, 25 files); everything that is not building reads one projection.
- **Built** (DESIGN.md 6.5.1, 9.5, 9.8, 9.9, 12 "Layout 9"): (1) **`ProjectModel`** (`DbDataBuild.Models`): name, model kind (any of the seven), connections (the project's default when none), `ObjectInfo` (tags, properties), definition file, query file, columns, grain; read-only; `ProjectContext.Models`. (2) **One selector grammar**, `ModelSelection` over `ProjectModel`s and the `DependencyGraph` (`SelectionHost` brings the connections for the hint and the one term that needs git, `changed:`); `ProjectContext.SelectModels` gives every kind and is what `project show metadata`, `project show graph`, `connection publish` and `project update` use; `ProjectContext.Select` is the same selection narrowed to the built models, for the commands that build, and says so when a selection names only declared models. `UpdateSelection.MatchDescriptors` is gone (it was a copy of the grammar); name patterns (`staging.*`) now work in every command; `+model` and `@model` include the declared models under it; `changed:` selects a changed mapped or native model and what reads it. `ModelSelectionTests` has the terms through both entry points and a scan that fails when `tag:` or `property:` is parsed in a second file. (The refresh's `SelectFromGraph` is still a second copy of the three terms it answers: subject 5 of this run replaces it.) (3) **One subject kind**: `metadata_document.kind` is **`subject_kind`** (`project`, `model`, `plan`), the rows of a mapped or native model are `model` rows in `metadata_document` and `object_info`, and `model_kind` carries all seven kinds. **Tracking layout 9**: `init --upgrade --apply` renames the column (`sp_rename`, `RENAME COLUMN`), turns the `source` rows into `model` rows (a `source` `object_info` row whose subject also has a `model` row is dropped first so the key cannot collide) and makes the views; `init` alone alters nothing and, on a layout 8 store, makes no views (they need the column) and records no version. (4) **One document**: `dbdatabuild.model/3` for all seven kinds (`kind`, `kind_settings`, `files`, `columns` with native types per connection, `indexes`, `foreign_keys`, `upstream`; a declared model has no query, loads, hooks, lineage or advice, which are left out); `dbdatabuild.source/2` is gone, and so is `consumers` (who reads a model is `SELECT subject FROM metadata_dependency WHERE depends_on = ? AND depth = 1`). The project document is `dbdatabuild.project/2`: one `models[]` (name, kind, file, connections, hash). (5) **One array**: `project compile` and `project show metadata` have `data.models[]` for every kind, no `sources[]`; `counts` has `models` for all seven and `by_kind`; the text says `OK: 12 model(s) valid (3 view, 2 full, 7 mapped)`. (6) **Views**: `metadata_models` for every kind (`kind`), `metadata_indexes` and the new `metadata_foreign_keys`; `metadata_sources`, `metadata_source_indexes`, `metadata_source_foreign_keys` and `metadata_columns.kind` (now `model_kind`) are gone; `metadata_lineage` is the built models only (a declared model has none). (7) **Graph**: a node has `category` (`model`, `macro`, `unknown`) and, for a model, `kind` (any of the seven: a mapped model is `mapped`, not `source`); `model_kind` is gone from the node; the diagram shapes follow the kind; the text line counts models by kind. (8) **Interfaces**: the TUI Models window and the web page list all seven kinds (a kind label on declared models; a Definition tab where a built model has Query and Lowered), and `project sample` names tables by model kind (`--sources` is `--declared`, `rows_per_source` is `rows_per_declared_model`). (9) **Names in the code**: `ModelSource` is `BuiltModel`, `SourceDescriptor` is `DeclaredModel` (`DeclaredModelLoader`, `DeclaredModelWriter`), `SourceForeignKey` is `ForeignKeyDefinition`, `ProjectValidationResult.Sources/Descriptors/NativeModels/FileDescriptors/AllDescriptors` are `BuiltModels/MappedModels/NativeModels/DeclaredModels/DeclaredModelsAndStaging`, `LoadedModel.Source` is `.Model`, `ImportSourcesCommand`, `SourceImport`, `SourceTypes`, `SourceChange` and the DDB-226/227/228 catalog names say mapped model, `schemas/source.schema.json` is `declared-model.schema.json`, the `sources` JSON keys of `project update`, `project model update` and `project import` are `mapped_models` and their row `kind` (table or view) is `object_kind`, `sources_without_a_seed` is `declared_models_without_a_seed`. `source` is left where it means a data source (`SourceCatalogReader`, a `source` resolver, a parameter's source, `FlightSqlRowSource`).
- **Templates**: the rules say `model_kind NOT IN ('mapped', 'native')` where they meant built models, the two layer rules are `marts_do_not_read_mapped_models.sql` and `staging_is_views_over_mapped_models.sql`, "every model declares its grain" skips declared models as before.
- **What broke for anyone using the old names** (nothing here was released): `metadata_current.kind` and `metadata_document.kind` are `subject_kind`; `metadata_current.kind = 'source'` rows are `model` rows; `metadata_columns.kind` is `model_kind`; the views `metadata_sources`, `metadata_source_indexes`, `metadata_source_foreign_keys` are gone (use `metadata_models`, `metadata_indexes`, `metadata_foreign_keys`); `dbdatabuild.source/N` documents and `consumers` are gone; `data.sources[]` of `project compile` and `project show metadata`, `data.project.sources[]`, and the `sources` key of the update and import documents; the graph node `kind` of `source`/`model`/`native`; the JSON `kind` of `connection publish` and `metadata_stored` rows (now `subject_kind`); `project sample --sources`; a selector now also selects declared models (so `tag:x` and `connection:c` can name a mapped model, and `render` on a selection of only declared models is an error instead of "selects no model").
- **Tests**: unit 2346 passed (new `ModelSelectionTests`: every term through `SelectModels` and `project update`, exclusion, built narrowing, the projection, the grammar scan; the layout, document, view, schema, counts, graph, sample and `changed:` tests moved to the new shapes). Conformance on SQL Server 2022 and PostgreSQL 17 (Flight SQL servers up): `apply` 205 passed, 3 skipped (the two timing tests and SQL Server 2025) and 6 failed on test expectations (the layout 5 and 7 migration simulations still had layout 8 versions, the import test read `kind`), fixed; the layout 7 to 8 test now renames the column back, and a new `A_store_of_layout_eight_gets_the_subject_kind_column...` runs the 8 to 9 migration on both engines (`sp_rename` and `RENAME COLUMN`, `source` rows to `model` rows in both tables, the views, repeatable, a publish after it writes nothing); those and the rest (`templates`, quick, `release`, the import test) passed: 78 passed, 6 skipped in the second run.
- **Not verified**: Fabric (the layout 9 upgrade is refused there as before); a store whose `metadata_document` holds a `source` and a `model` row for the same name and the same `recorded_utc` (the upgrade would fail on the key; the clock is monotonic per process, so it needs two processes in one millisecond); the TUI and web page were not driven by hand (the web page is a static file, the TUI reads the same JSON); the documents a store holds from before keep their old text until the next publish.
- **Decisions for the owner**: (1) `metadata_document.kind` is renamed `subject_kind` (the review only asked for the value change; the name matches `object_info.subject_kind` and leaves `kind` free to mean the model kind everywhere). (2) `consumers` is dropped from the documents rather than carried on every model (it changes when another model changes, which made a document depend on the rest of the project); the dependency rows answer it. (3) A declared model's document carries `native` types for its connections (a rule over "columns whose postgres type is X" now sees mapped models too). (4) A selection of only declared models for a command that builds is an error that says why, not an empty success. (5) The graph's node `category`/`kind` are decided here; subject 2 gives the dependency rows the same two columns.

## 168. Dependency kinds: the model kind of a dependency, and a category for what is no model (tracking layout 9; owner direction 2026-10-14; OPEN-ITEMS section V)

- **Found first**: `depends_on_kind` (and `upstream_kind`, a document's `upstream[].kind`) lumped the five built kinds as `model` and used the same column for things that are no model (`macro`, `unknown`), so a rule could not ask "views only" without joining, and a column that sometimes said a model kind and sometimes a sort of thing had two meanings. `loads.yml` already carried specific model kinds on both sides of an edge (entry 166, the rows are between two loaded models), so it needed no change.
- **Built** (DESIGN.md 9.8, 12 "Dependency kinds (layout 9)"; `docs/research/cross-server.md` Terms): two columns, two words. **`depends_on_category`** says what sort of thing a dependency is: `model`, `macro` or `unknown` (never null). **`depends_on_kind`** is the *model kind* when it is a model, any of the seven (`view`, `full`, `incremental_by_unique_key`, `incremental_by_time_range`, `copy`, `mapped`, `native`), and null for a macro or an unknown table. The word is `category` because `kind` is the model kind and `type` is a column's SQL type, and the owner's rule is one word per concept; the Terms table has the two rows ("Model kind", "Dependency category"). The same two names are `category` and `kind` in a model document's `upstream[]` (`dbdatabuild.model/3`: `{name, category, kind}`, `kind` absent for a macro or an unknown table) and in a graph node (entry 167), and `upstream_category`/`upstream_kind` and `ancestor_category`/`ancestor_kind` in the views `metadata_upstream` and `metadata_ancestors`. `DependencyRow(Subject, DependsOn, DependsOnCategory, DependsOnKind?, Depth)`; `MetadataDependencies.Closure` takes the category and the kind of each edge; the comparison that finds the subjects to rewrite includes both. A copy's generated staging table counts as a mapped model, as before.
- **Layout 9, the second change**: in `metadata_dependency`, `depends_on_category` is a new column (not null) and `depends_on_kind` may be null. `init` creates the table in the new shape and does not record 9 on a store whose table lacks the column; `init --upgrade --apply` empties the table (its rows are derived from the project), makes `depends_on_kind` nullable and adds the column (`ALTER COLUMN` / `ADD`, `DO` block on PostgreSQL), and the next `publish` writes the rows again (it reports `dependencies of N model(s) written`). Layout 9 has not been released, so this is one layout, not two.
- **Templates**: `staging_is_views_over_mapped_models.sql` says `u.upstream_kind IS DISTINCT FROM 'mapped'` (a macro now has a null kind; `<>` would let it through); `marts_do_not_read_mapped_models.sql` is unchanged (`upstream_kind = 'mapped'`).
- **What broke**: a rule that said `upstream_kind = 'model'` or `depends_on_kind = 'model'` says `upstream_category = 'model' AND upstream_kind NOT IN ('mapped', 'native')` (or the kind it means: `'view'`); a rule that tested `upstream_kind = 'macro'` or `'unknown'` tests `upstream_category`; `<>` on a kind that can now be null needs `IS DISTINCT FROM`; a tool reading `upstream[].kind` from a document sees `category` beside it.
- **Tests**: unit 2346 passed (closure with the seven kinds, a macro and an unknown table in the rows, the documents' `upstream[]`, the in-memory views, the macro test's rows with category `macro` and null kind, the schema; the layout test). Conformance on SQL Server 2022 and PostgreSQL 17 (Flight SQL servers up), all groups after subjects 1 and 2 together: 287 passed, 9 skipped (the two timing tests, SQL Server 2025, the extra-engine ones), 0 failed; the 8 to 9 migration test now also covers the dependency table (a layout 8 shape with no category column and a not-null kind is emptied, made right, and a publish writes `marts.v1>src.t|model|mapped|1` again; a macro row with a null kind inserts).
- **Not verified**: Fabric; a store with rows published by a layout 9 build before this entry (the upgrade only meets layout 8 tables; a layout 9 store made by the commit before this one lacks the category column and `init` does not record the version on it, so `init --upgrade --apply` repairs it: not tried on a real store).

## 169. The old `kind: {type: ...}` form is removed (owner direction 2026-10-14; OPEN-ITEMS section V)

- **Why**: entry 163 kept the first form of `kind` loading for one release, with a warning and a rewrite. Nothing had been released with only the new form, so there was nobody to migrate; the warning path, the rewriter and the doubled loader branches were dead weight the owner asked to drop.
- **Built** (DESIGN.md 6.1): a `kind` mapping with a `type` key is **DDB-248** (an error) in a model, a mapped or native model, a folder's `_dbdatabuild.yml` or the project file, with a message that names the form to write (`kind: {copy: {...}}` with the other keys as the settings, or `kind: copy` when it has none). Gone: `KindFormRewriter`, the old-form branches of `ModelKindShape` (`OldKey`, `IsOldForm`, `KindReading.OldForm`), `ProjectValidator.WarnOldKindForm`, the old-form scan, rewrite and confirmation of `project model update` (`FindOldKindForms`, `RewriteOldKindForms`), the `old_kind_forms` key of the `project model update` and `project update` documents (and the output schema), the `--check` count of old-form files, and the sentence in the command's description and the skill. **DDB-247** (the warning) is retired: it stays in the catalog (title "Retired: ...", explanation of what it was and that DDB-248 replaced it) so the code is never given another meaning; nothing raises it, and a test says so. The editor schemas already refused the old form.
- **What broke**: a project that still writes `kind: {type: ...}` (anywhere) no longer compiles; `project model update` no longer rewrites it (do it by hand: the message says how). `data.old_kind_forms` is gone from two documents.
- **Tests**: unit 2335 passed (`ModelKindShapeTests` loses the old-form-loads, warn-once, byte-identical rendering and rewriter tests and has the refusal in four shapes, a folder-file case through `project compile`, and a new-form project that `project model update --check` finds clean; the fuzz test for the kind loses the rewriter half; fixtures that still wrote the old form (`GraphTests`, `HistoryConcernsTests`, `ModelDefinitionTests`, `YamlMergeTests`) are in the new form). The conformance projects write only the new form (entry 163 converted them), so they were not run again for this subject; they run at the end of the run.
- **Not verified**: none beyond the above; Fabric is unaffected.

## 170. `project show refresh` shows the dependency graph of `loads.yml` (OPEN-ITEMS section U)

- **Built** (DESIGN.md 10.6 "Seeing the graph"): for each plan, the edges of the loads file between the plan's members: text (a line saying how many edges, and "waits for: marts.fct_events [incremental_by_time_range] (2 steps)" under each member), JSON `plans[].dependencies` (`model`, `model_kind`, `depends_on`, `depends_on_kind`, `depth`, all of them) and, with a plan named, `load_members[].waits_for`. A loads file without the graph gives null and the text says why. `schemas/output.schema.json` (closed `data_project_show_refresh`), the command description and so `docs/commands.md`, `docs/operations.md`.
- **Interfaces**: the terminal interface runs `project show refresh` from its command catalog and shows the text; neither it nor the web page has a refresh-plan screen (they show deploy plans), so there was nothing else to extend.

## 171. `loads.yml` stores the facts selectors read; a refresh selects with every term but `changed:` without the project (OPEN-ITEMS section U)

- **Built** (DESIGN.md 10.6 "The facts selectors read", the refresh paragraph): (1) **`loads.yml` has `models: N` and a `models:` list**, one row per loaded model: name, model kind, connections, tags, properties (key, type, text), definition file, query file. Sorted, no timestamps, no machine names, hashed with the file (an edited, removed, recounted or renamed row is refused like an edited plan), and it must list exactly the loaded models. A file compiled before has no `models` header and reads as having none. (2) **One grammar for the refresh**: `ModelSelection` now takes an `IModelGraph` (the project's `DependencyGraph` and the file's `LoadGraph` both implement it), so `RefreshCommand.Select` runs the same code over `ModelFacts.ToProjectModel()` rows and `LoadGraph`; `SelectFromGraph`, its three-term copy of the grammar, `GlobModels` and `LoadGraph.Expand` are gone, and the refresh gained what the grammar has (`tag:`, `property:`, `connection:`, `path:`, files, directories, patterns, `@`) without the project. (3) **What still needs the project, exactly**: `changed:` (the working tree and the revision history: a hook script, macro file, folder file, native text or rendered file counts for the models it reaches, and none of that is in the file), and every term when the file has no facts (compiled before). File and directory terms are answered from the stored paths; no filesystem is read. `RefreshCommand` loads the project for nothing else. (4) A name that is a model of the project but no load of the file is now an error that says "among the models this loads file loads" instead of selecting nothing. (5) No other command selects from a compiled plan (`project show refresh` takes a plan name, `deploy`/`apply` run a plan file), so there was nothing else to move.
- **Tests**: unit 2338 passed (`LoadGraphTests`: 16 terms (tag, property with and without a value, path, connection, kinds, pattern, file, directory, `+`, `@`, trailing `+`, intersection, exclusion) give the same members from the facts with the models folder deleted as from the project; `tag:nothing` and an unloaded model say so; `changed:` falls to the project; the facts are deterministic and refused when edited, recounted or not the loaded models). Conformance: `RefreshParallelConformanceTests` runs a pattern, `kind:full`, `+marts.d1` and an exclusion with the models folder parked, `--parallel 3`, effective 3 (below).
- **Not verified**: Fabric; `changed:` in a refresh against a real repository (it takes the project path of entry 166, unchanged); very large projects (the rows are one per loaded model).
