## 173. The lowerer reads DuckDB 2.0's own plans; `delim_join_as_cte` is no longer set (owner request 2026-10-14; OPEN-ITEMS section H)

- **Why**: the 2026-10-02 adaptation made 2.0 plan like 1.x by setting `delim_join_as_cte = false`. The owner asked for the translations instead, with 1.5.6 (DuckDB.NET) staying the default and passing everything.
- **What "latest 2.0" is** (2026-10-15): `v2.0.0-alpha48141`, source `c457670e67`, branch head `bde1b27b4610`; shared-library sha256 28d58fa78efd82d305aed467f4a2d5e5b511e5b5c1dff0f75cc269d8cc6b62ff (tarball d2ac5ec5...). duckdb/duckdb's latest release is still v1.5.6; DuckDB.NET's latest is 1.5.6 (no 2.0 package), so the package pin is unchanged and 2.0 is tried by swapping the native library (`scripts/test-duckdb-preview.sh`).
- **Built** (DESIGN.md 7, "Plan format and DuckDB versions"; the table of shapes is in `docs/research/duckdb-2.0/README.md`): `QueryDescriber.PreparePlanConnection` is deleted (on 1.5 the setting does not exist, so nothing changes there). `PlanNormalizer.UndoDelimCtes` turns 2.0's materialized-CTE form of a delim join back into `LOGICAL_DELIM_JOIN` (an exact inverse of what DuckDB does, so the count-bug guard and everything the 1.x code read works unchanged); `PlanLowerer.PushCorrelation` puts a correlated `=` that 2.0 moved into a join condition back inside the subquery at the place of its `IS NOT NULL` (used by keyed SINGLE/LEFT/INNER joins, `TryKeyedLimit` for `ORDER BY ... LIMIT k`, `MarkJoin`, the count patch and delim joins); SEMI joins to the outer values; a LEFT join to an ungrouped aggregate is a scalar subquery; `left_projection_map`; join conditions that are predicates over both sides; `string_agg`/quantile arguments; UNPIVOT names; interval constants. ANY/ALL in 2.0's CASE form is refused; a row-value `IN` follows the standard's NULL logic on a 2.0 plan. Every inversion is done only when exact; otherwise the join to a derived table stays (same rows) or the query is refused.
- **Verified**: lowered text 2.0 against 1.5.6 on the 126 corpus queries: 123 identical (the others are information 2.0 drops, README), equal to what 2.0 gave under the old setting for all 126; 52 subquery forms: 48 identical. Unit on **1.5.6: 2338 passed** (plus a scratch probe that is not committed); on **alpha48141: 2336 passed, 2 failed** (below). Conformance (SQL Server 2022 and PostgreSQL 17; all groups in one run, Flight SQL servers not up): on **1.5.6 279 passed, 17 skipped, 0 failed**; on **alpha48141 274 passed, 5 failed**: the adventureworks mart `rpt_product_revenue_spread` on PostgreSQL (the decimal quantile last digit), the probe tests on both engines (`%` and `//` by zero raise in 2.0 so the oracle errors; `dp_week` on PostgreSQL and `date_sub_dates` on SQL Server answer differently, not investigated), and the ANY/ALL rows test (refused on 2.0).
- **Not translated / open on 2.0**: ANY/ALL; `current_date()` in a macro default is folded to a literal at bind time (the lowered text would freeze the lowering day; `MacroTests.A_macro_with_default_parameters...` fails on the preview and this is the finding that blocks adopting 2.0); `%` and `//` by zero (the unit oracle sets `error_on_division_by_zero = false`); decimal `median`/`quantile_cont` last digit (`Quantiles_of_decimals...` fails on the preview).
- **Not verified**: Fabric (never run); 2.0 on Windows; the preview CI (no workflow runs it, so none was changed).

## 174. The planning connections keep the clock calls: volatile replacements of the built-ins DuckDB 2.0 folds (owner request 2026-10-15; OPEN-ITEMS section H)

- **Why**: entry 173 left one finding that made 2.0 unsafe: the binder folds stable-within-query functions (`current_date`, `now()` ...) into constants inside other calls, optimizer off or not, so a lowered query froze the day of lowering (`CAST('2026-10-15' AS DATE) - INTERVAL '7 DAY'`). The owner proved (C API scratch programs) that registering a volatile scalar function under a built-in's name replaces it on that instance and the plan then keeps the call.
- **Built** (DESIGN.md 7, "The clock in the plan"; the analysis, the list of what folds and the caveats are in `docs/research/duckdb-2.0/README.md`, last section): `ClockOverrides.json` (the 12 overrides with types and how each computes the real value, and what is not covered) and `ClockOverrides` (registration through DuckDB.NET.Bindings, which address the same native library, the swapped preview included); `PlanningConnection.Open`, the one place that opens a planning connection (fresh in-memory instance, locked down, replacements registered only where this DuckDB folds the clock, found once per process by planning `year(current_date)`; verified once; fails soft on 1.x, fails closed on 2.x: `SerializePlan` returns an error rather than a plan that freezes the day); `QueryDescriber.Describe`, `SerializePlan`, `CheckPrelude` go through it (so do the plan-and-answer connections of `PlanLowererTests`, `TargetRulesTests` and `EngineProbe`). Not given the override, on purpose: the `sample` and `test` runs, `InputExpressions` (its own frozen-clock macros), the DuckDB origin, `MetadataDatabase`, the parse-only `DuckParseTree`, `DuckDbVersion`. **DDB-325** (new): a macro default that is a clock or session function, which DuckDB now refuses itself ("not a constant expression"), reported with the fix, as a warning at the definition and an error where a model reaches it. Lowering: `transaction_timestamp()` is `now()`; `localtime` is `CAST(current_localtimestamp() AS TIME)` (polyglot wrote `CURRENT_LOCALTIME()`, which neither engine has); `version()`, `txid_current()`, `current_schema()`, `current_schemas()`, `current_database()`, `current_connection_id()`, `current_query()`, `current_query_id()`, `current_transaction_id()`, `current_setting()` and `getenv()` are refused (DDB-324) where `version()` and `txid_current()` used to reach SQL Server as functions it does not have. `DBDATABUILD_DUCKDB_CLOCK_OVERRIDES=always|never` and `PlanningConnection.UseMode` force the choice (tests).
- **Verified**: lowered text of the nested calls (`d > current_date - INTERVAL 7 DAY`, `date_trunc('month', current_date)`, `current_date + i`, `now() - INTERVAL 1 HOUR`, `year(current_date) - year(d)`, `date_diff('day', d, current_date)`, `current_timestamp`, `transaction_timestamp()`, `localtime`) equals one fixed 1.5.6 text on SQL Server and PostgreSQL on **both** DuckDB builds; the replacements have the built-ins' types and values (against a plain connection); lowered DuckDB text run on a plain connection gives the rows of the original (5 queries over 15 dated rows). Unit on **1.5.6: 2364 passed** (2338 before; 26 new); on **v2.0.0-alpha48256: 2363 passed, 1 failed** (the decimal quantile last digit; `MacroTests.A_macro_with_default_parameters...` now passes). Conformance (SQL Server 2022, PostgreSQL 17; the new `ClockConformanceTests`: 13 queries compared with DuckDB plus the engine's own clock, no date literal in the rendered text): on **1.5.6 quick 65 passed / 6 skipped, apply 211 passed / 3 skipped, templates 15 passed, release 4 passed, 0 failed**; on **alpha48256 quick 63 passed / 2 failed, apply 209 passed / 2 failed**: the failures are the ones entry 173 listed (the probe test: `%`, `//` and floor division by zero raise in the oracle, `dp_week` on PostgreSQL, `date_sub_dates` on SQL Server; the ANY/ALL and row-value `IN` test on both engines) and no clock test fails. With `DBDATABUILD_DUCKDB_CLOCK_OVERRIDES=never` on the alpha `ClockConformanceTests` fails with the frozen `CAST('2026-10-15' AS DATE)` (which is the proof the test discriminates). templates and release were not run on the alpha.
- **Not verified**: Windows and macOS native-library names (the bindings resolve them; untested), Fabric (never run), a DuckDB library lacking the scalar-function C API (the fail-closed path is covered by a unit test of the status only), DuckDB.NET on 2.0 (no build).
- **Does not cover**: `current_schemas`, `current_setting`, `getvariable`, `getenv` (their results cannot be replaced by a registered function; they still fold on 2.0), a connection whose TimeZone changes after it is opened, `age()` (reaches SQL Server as `AGE(...)`, which it lacks; unchanged).
- **Decisions for the owner**: (1) the override is on automatically only where the library folds (1.x untouched); say if it should be always on. (2) A clock or session function as a macro default is now an error where it was accepted on 1.x only when the override is on (2.0, or forced); 1.5 still accepts it. (3) Refusing the session functions changes the text for `version()` and `txid_current()` on 1.5 (they were invalid on SQL Server). (4) `age()` and `current_time` on SQL Server (a time without the zone) were left as they were.

## 175. The function matrix: every native DuckDB function on SQL Server and PostgreSQL, measured (owner request 2026-10-15, part A; OPEN-ITEMS section W)

- **Why**: the owner asked which DuckDB functions do not work on SQL Server and PostgreSQL, built from data and not by hand, after seeing `age(...)` reach SQL Server as `AGE(...)`.
- **Built** (no change to the tool): `scripts/function-matrix.sh` runs the conformance test `FunctionMatrixRun` (`tests/DbDataBuild.Tests.Conformance/FunctionMatrix/`) and then `scripts/function-matrix-report.py`. The test lists `duckdb_functions()` of the DuckDB the tool ships (1.5.6): 2767 overloads of 708 functions (scalar, aggregate, window, operators labelled by the symbol, the macros of `main` and `pg_catalog`); builds one minimal query per overload from its parameter types (columns of the probe table cast to the type; literals where an argument is a date part, a pattern, a format or a JSON path, one variant per valid spelling); runs the **same calls as the compile** (the bound plan, `PlanLowerer`, `TargetRules`, the matrix lint, the transpile, the target's parser: `StaticPipeline`) for SQL Server (version 16), PostgreSQL and Fabric text; and runs what lowers on the real engines against DuckDB's rows, one sample row at a time when the whole set fails, with intervals compared as (months, days, microseconds). Output: `docs/research/function-matrix.csv` (one row per function, aliases together), `function-matrix-overloads.csv` (one row per overload, with the texts sent), `function-matrix-table-functions.csv` (listed, not probed), `function-matrix-meta.txt`, and the generated `function-matrix.md` (method, caveats, counts, the matrix, the cross-check with `matrix/constructs.yml`); the findings section is `scripts/function-matrix-findings.md`.
- **Verified** (SQL Server 2022 RTM-CU26 and PostgreSQL 17.11, docker, loopback): the dynamic outcomes in the data. **Not run**: Fabric (the column says only whether its text equals SQL Server's); SQL Server 2025.
- **Found** (docs/research/function-matrix.md, "Findings"): silent wrong values, among them `log(x)` on SQL Server (natural log for DuckDB's base 10), `date + INTERVAL '1 month 2 days ...'` on SQL Server (days and time dropped), `md5`/`sha256`/`bit_length` over text on SQL Server (UTF-16), `date_trunc('week')` and `date_part('week')` on SQL Server, `date_diff('decade'|'century'|'millennium')` on PostgreSQL (an `AGE(...)` interval), `strftime` formats on both, 32-bit FLOAT computed in 64-bit; bitwise operators, `**`, `^`, `!` and `^@` fail in the transpile's parse (7 functions); about 180 functions on SQL Server and 160 on PostgreSQL go out under DuckDB's name and fail at the engine with only DDB-305 (a warning) at compile.
- **Not changed** (decisions for the owner, in the findings): a `log` rule, interval expansion, the parse failures, matrix rows for the missing functions, FLOAT mapping, tables for date parts and formats.
- **Tests**: the new test is skipped without `DDB_FUNCTION_MATRIX_OUT`; it asserts nothing about the results (they are data).

## 176. `age()` on SQL Server and PostgreSQL: scoped and measured, not built (owner request 2026-10-15, part B; OPEN-ITEMS W-2)

- **Why**: `age(...)` reaches SQL Server as `AGE(...)`, which does not exist there. The owner asked for the semantics, the shapes models use it in, and what to translate, refuse or leave.
- **Built**: nothing in the tool. `scripts/age-probe.sh` runs the conformance test `AgeProbe` (`FunctionMatrix/AgeProbe.cs`) and writes `docs/research/age-function-results.txt`; the study is `docs/research/age-function.md`.
- **Verified** (DuckDB 1.5.6, SQL Server 2022 16.0.4265, PostgreSQL 17.11) on 5449 ordered pairs (43 edge dates, every pair, and 60 timestamps with six times of day, both directions, month ends, the leap days of 2000 and 2024, the non-leap 1900 and 2100): (1) **PostgreSQL's native `age` equals DuckDB's** as (months, days, microseconds) on all 5449; (2) a **T-SQL formulation written inline** (the borrowing rule: whole months from `DATEDIFF(MONTH)` minus a borrow, days with the days of the earlier month, microseconds from the time of day) equals DuckDB's on all 5449 for the interval itself, `year`, `month`, `day`, `hour`, `minute`, `second`, and the comparisons with an interval (`>`, `>=`, `=`, `<`, 5449 each) and between two ages (1600 of 1600); (3) the differences found: PostgreSQL's `extract(second FROM age)` is fractional (2400 of 5449 differ; `trunc(...)` fixes it), the lowered comparison text carries DuckDB's `normalized_interval(...)`, which neither engine has (PostgreSQL: `function normalized_interval(interval) does not exist`), and a bigint divisor is needed for the hour on SQL Server (a plain literal divides as decimal).
- **What the tool sends today** (measured): SQL Server fails for every shape with `'AGE' is not a recognized built-in function name` (static `same`, only DDB-305); PostgreSQL works for value, year, month, day, hour and minute, differs for second, and fails for every comparison.
- **Recommendation and cost**: translate the exactly portable shapes for SQL Server and Fabric (a rule `age-parts`), strip `normalized_interval` and truncate the second on PostgreSQL, refuse the rest through a matrix row (DDB-301; DDB-324 is the target-neutral lowerer's and cannot refuse for one target); about one working session, listed in the document, section 7 and 8. **Not verified**: Fabric (never run), TIMESTAMPTZ, years outside 1900 to 2100, SQL Server 2025.


## 177. The function gaps: what the function matrix found is translated, refused with a name, or measured and left (owner decisions 2026-10-15; OPEN-ITEMS section W)

- **Why**: entry 175 measured every DuckDB function on SQL Server and PostgreSQL and listed silent wrong answers, lowerer failures and about 180 and 160 functions that fail at the engine with only DDB-305. The owner decided that a functionally equivalent rewrite is welcome and preferred over a refusal, and that nothing is approximated silently.
- **Built, one commit per item** (rules in `DbDataBuild.Targets/Rules`: `TargetRules.cs`, `FunctionRules.cs`, `FunctionTemplates.cs` (the table), `AgeRules.cs`, `StatisticsRules.cs`; lowering in `PlanLowerer.cs` and `TextFormats.cs`; catalog in `RewriteCatalog`):
  1. `log-base-ten` (SQL Server, Fabric: `log(x)` is `LOG10`), `log-with-base` (PostgreSQL: `log(b, x)` is `ln(x) / ln(b)`, which is how DuckDB computes it), matrix row `fn.log`.
  2. `interval-dateadd` (SQL Server, Fabric): `date + INTERVAL '1 month 2 days 03:04:05'`, an interval in front of the date, and sums of constant intervals become one DATEADD per part, months then days then the time; the reader `Core/IntervalParts` is checked against DuckDB on 3000 random texts; a DATE gets a timestamp cast before a time unit (the lowerer's `CAST(... AS TIMESTAMP)` is the mark); `to_weeks`, `to_quarters`, `to_decades` and the other folded units lower (they failed on both engines); matrix row `op.interval_text` for text the reader does not know exactly.
  3. `&`, `|`, `~`, `^` (power, a double), `**`, `^@`, postfix `!`, `xor`, `<<`, `>>`: the lowerer writes them in a form the parser reads (the parse failure was in the lowered text, not in polyglot: no upstream change); rules `bit-operators`, `shift-left`, `shift-right` (exact arithmetic: a product in DECIMAL(38, 0) cast back, and a floor division by a power of two with the complement for negatives; SQL Server 2022's `LEFT_SHIFT` wraps and `RIGHT_SHIFT` fills with zeros, so they are not used), `factorial-table` (the 34 values as DECIMAL(38, 0), an overflow beyond); a whole-number constant of `power` is written as a double (T-SQL `POWER(2, -1)` was 0). Rows `op.shift`, `fn.factorial`, `fn.xor`, `fn.power`.
  4. `FunctionTemplates` (a table, each row run against DuckDB, as text and on both engines): cosh, sinh, tanh, asinh, acosh, atanh (SQL Server), cbrt, atan2, log2, dayname, monthname, ends_with, suffix, prefix, isnan, isinf, last_day, gcd and lcm names; 22 rows `fn.missing.<theme>[.sqlserver|.postgres]` refuse the other 183 functions at compile (DDB-301) with the function named; the matrix message now names the call (`MatrixLinter.Describe`); `normalized_interval` is a row.
  5. FLOAT: the cast was REAL already (true for a cast in a select list only; see entry 179 for column definitions, VALUES cells and `FLOAT(n)`); the polyglot division rewrite widened a FLOAT quotient to 64 bits, `round(REAL, n)` multiplied in REAL on SQL Server and PostgreSQL has no `round(real, integer)` nor `trunc(double precision, integer)`: FLOAT division, `round` and `trunc` are computed in double and cut back to FLOAT; rule `trunc-digits`.
  6. `date-part-units`, `date-trunc-units`, `date-diff-units` (week and isoyear, yearweek, decade, century, millennium, era, julian, epoch, millisecond and microsecond as DuckDB counts them, whole seconds on PostgreSQL, time units of a DATE, `DATEDIFF_BIG`, PostgreSQL's rounded `date_diff('second')` made a boundary count); `strftime` (24 specifiers) and `format` (`{}` only) are written out as text by the lowerer; everything else is refused naming the unit (`fn.date_unit`) or the specifier (DDB-324). Plan names `julian(x)`, `epoch(x)`, `era(x)`, `weekofyear(x)` ... are read as `date_part`.
  7. `age-parts` (SQL Server, Fabric), `normalized-interval` and `age-second` (PostgreSQL), row `fn.age` (an age as a value, in arithmetic, with epoch or sub-second parts, over an operand with a subquery or a random value: DDB-301; TIMESTAMPTZ: DDB-324 from the lowerer). `covariance-exact` (PostgreSQL): covar_pop, covar_samp and regr_sxy from exact NUMERIC sums (-334 and -16384 for 0 next to a 1e10 outlier).
- **Verified** on SQL Server 2022 (16.0.4265) and PostgreSQL 17.11: `FunctionRuleProbes` (about 330 cases over the probe table and edge literals, numbers compared to a relative 1e-11), `AgeRuleConformance` (961 ordered pairs of edge timestamps times 12 shapes, both engines), and the unit differentials against DuckDB (`FunctionRulesTests`). Known and documented: where DuckDB raises for a shift (negative value, count out of range) the engine returns the product or raises on overflow; Infinity and NaN from `power`, `atanh(1)` and the like are an error or NULL on the engines; hyperbolic functions agree to about 12 digits and `cbrt` to the last digit or two.
- **Not verified**: Fabric (never run; every Fabric matrix entry of the new rows is `unverified`), SQL Server 2025, TIMESTAMPTZ arguments, years outside 1000 to 9999, `FLOAT` columns (the cast form was run).
- **Refused instead of translated** (and why): `age` as a value (no SQL Server type), epoch and sub-second parts of an age, `strftime` week numbers, zones and locale formats, `format` width and type specifications and DOUBLE arguments, shifts over TINYINT (unsigned on SQL Server), HUGEINT and the unsigned types, `date_part`, `date_trunc` and `date_diff` units not in the table, 183 functions with no counterpart (aggregates, lists, encodings, similarity, JSON, UUIDs, statistics).

## 178. VALUES lists: DuckDB's constant table is lowered to the table value constructor (OPEN-ITEMS W-3)

- **Why**: a `VALUES (...)` list, inline or as `(VALUES ...) AS t(a, b)`, failed in the lowerer with `EXPRESSION_GET` (entry 177 listed it as left), and `AgeRuleConformance` worked around it with `UNION ALL` groups of eight.
- **Built**: `PlanLowerer.ValuesList` lowers `LOGICAL_EXPRESSION_GET` to `(VALUES (...), ...) AS vals(col0, col1, ...)` (DuckDB's own column names; the projection above renames). Every cell is lowered by the ordinary expression lowering; while a cell is lowered `exactCasts` keeps the cast of a constant (SQL Server types a bare `7` as INT, `2147483648` and `1.5` as NUMERIC and `TRUE` as 1): only VARCHAR and INTEGER literals stay bare, a FLOAT cell is written REAL, a NULL is `CAST(NULL AS type)`, and a boolean expression is `CAST(CASE WHEN e THEN TRUE WHEN NOT e THEN FALSE END AS BOOLEAN)`. Target rules for SQL Server and Fabric (catalog entries, so in rendered headers): `values-chunks` (more than 1000 rows: `SELECT cols FROM (VALUES <=1000 rows) AS g(cols)` branches joined by UNION ALL inside the derived table) and `values-selects` (a row whose cell still holds an interval after the other rules, a date plus an interval or a number of days: a SELECT per row joined by UNION ALL, because the transpile writes DATEADD only in a select list and leaves `INTERVAL '1' MONTH` in a VALUES row). PostgreSQL gets the list as written. Matrix row `syntax.values_list` (SQL Server `translated`, Fabric `unverified`, PostgreSQL `native`), spike case `values_list`, fixture in `MatrixLinterTests`; the matrix hash changed and the goldens were regenerated with no difference. No new diagnostic code (refusals are DDB-324). `AgentKit/SKILL.md` says what works.
- **Refused (DDB-324, the type named)**: a column of type TINYINT (unsigned on SQL Server), UTINYINT to UBIGINT, HUGEINT, TIME, TIMESTAMP WITH TIME ZONE, INTERVAL, LIST, STRUCT, ENUM and the like; a list that reads another table's columns (`LATERAL (VALUES (t.a))`).
- **Tests, three ways**: `ValuesListTests` (64 cases: exact lowered text, the lowered query on DuckDB against the original for rows, column names and column types over NULLs, mixed int and decimal, BIGINT widening, FLOAT, doubles, text with quotes and non-BMP, dates, timestamps, booleans, boolean and interval cells, one row, joins, CTE, `IN`, `NOT IN`, `EXISTS`, scalar subquery, GROUP BY, ORDER BY LIMIT, UNION ALL, DISTINCT; the chunk counts for 1, 1000, 1001, 2500 and 5000 rows on SQL Server and Fabric and none on PostgreSQL; the chunked form on DuckDB for 1001 and 3500 rows and as a join side, CTE and `IN` subquery; the refusals) and `ValuesListConformance` (42 queries on SQL Server 2022 and PostgreSQL 17, rows compared to DuckDB's, column names, and column types from `sys.dm_exec_describe_first_result_set` and `pg_typeof`/`scale`, including lists of 999, 1000, 1001, 2500 and 5000 rows and large lists as a join side, in a CTE, in `IN` and in aggregates). `AgeRuleConformance` now uses one VALUES list of 31 timestamps per side of the cross join (961 pairs in one query per shape; the same coverage as the groups of eight).
- **Verified** on SQL Server 2022 and PostgreSQL 17 (docker): the unit suite (2680), the conformance groups quick, apply and templates/release (see the run line in the report for the last numbers). **Not verified**: Fabric (never run), SQL Server 2025, a long list (thousands of rows) whose cells hold an interval on SQL Server (written as one SELECT per row; not measured), TIME and TIMESTAMPTZ columns (refused).
- **Function matrix**: not re-run; no function of the matrix uses a VALUES list, so no count can change.

## 179. FLOAT is 32 bits on every target in every position: the rule `float-width` (OPEN-ITEMS W-10(a)); a correction of entry 177

- **Correction of entry 177 (and the claim in entry 178)**: entry 177 said the FLOAT cast "was REAL already". That is true of a cast in a select list, and measured again here: `CAST(x AS FLOAT)`, `TRY_CAST`, `x::FLOAT`, `x::FLOAT4`, `x::REAL` all transpile to `REAL` on SQL Server, Fabric and PostgreSQL, on the lowered path and the direct path, and a real-engine run of 37 shapes (cast of a DOUBLE, INTEGER, DECIMAL and text column, CTE, subquery, UNION ALL, CASE, COALESCE, arithmetic, `round`, sum, avg) gave REAL on both engines with DuckDB's values. Entry 178's note that a plain select-list cast "still becomes `float(53)`" could **not be reproduced** at this pin (`native-0a7a1a77`). What is wrong is every FLOAT that is not under a cast: the transpile writes a **column definition** `a FLOAT` as `FLOAT` (8 bytes on SQL Server) and a cast **inside a VALUES row** as `FLOAT` on SQL Server and Fabric, and it keeps `FLOAT(n)`, `FLOAT4` and `FLOAT8` as written: `CAST(x AS REAL(24))` and `CAST(x AS FLOAT8)` are not SQL Server, `REAL(24)` is not PostgreSQL, and `FLOAT(53)` became `REAL(53)`/`REAL`, a 32-bit type for a 64-bit one.
- **Built**: the target rule `float-width` (`TargetRules.FloatType`, catalog entry, SQL Server, Fabric and PostgreSQL, required, named in rendered headers when it changes a type) rewrites each type node in the DuckDB-dialect tree before the transpile: FLOAT, FLOAT4, REAL and `FLOAT(n)` for n 1 to 24 become the REAL spelling, which all three write as REAL; FLOAT8 and `FLOAT(n)` for 25 to 53 become DOUBLE (`FLOAT` on SQL Server and Fabric, `double precision` on PostgreSQL). This is the cause (the type nodes), not a text replace, and it does not look at values. DuckDB's own `FLOAT(n)` rule is the PostgreSQL/T-SQL one (measured on DuckDB 1.5.4: 1 to 24 FLOAT, 25 to 53 DOUBLE; 0 and 54 are parse errors "precision for type float must be at least 1 bit / less than 54 bits"), so the odd case is not refused: it is mapped as DuckDB reads it; a precision outside 1 to 53 never reaches the step (the model runs on DuckDB first) and is left alone. The special case in the lowerer for a FLOAT cell of a VALUES list (entry 178: the lowerer wrote `REAL`) is removed: the lowerer writes FLOAT and the rule does the rest. Already exact and unchanged: `Ddl.cs` (FLOAT is `real`, DOUBLE `float(53)`/`double precision`; a logical type `FLOAT(n)` is not a logical type and stays unmapped), the imported-type tables (`ImportedTypes`, `DialectProfiles`: SQL Server `real` is FLOAT, `float` is DOUBLE; PostgreSQL `real`/`float4` FLOAT, `double precision`/`float8` DOUBLE), `LoadSeedsCommand`, `MutationGate` and `Transfer` (Single / Real). A stale comment in `LogicalTypes` ("FLOAT is 8 bytes there") is corrected. No new diagnostic code, no matrix row; the matrix hash and all golden files are unchanged (the unit suite needed no `UPDATE_GOLDEN`).
- **Tests, three ways**: `FloatWidthTests` (text for the three targets over cast, TRY_CAST, `::`, FLOAT4, REAL, FLOAT(1/24/25/53), VALUES cell, column definitions; the rule fires only on a changed type; the DuckDB-refused precisions; the differential: the rule's DuckDB-dialect output run on DuckDB against the original for values and column types, including 0.1 and 16777217 through FLOAT to DOUBLE, `FLOAT(24)` against `FLOAT(25)`, and the lowered form of a FLOAT cast and a VALUES cell) and `FloatTypeConformance` on SQL Server 2022 and PostgreSQL 17 (37 queries with rows compared to DuckDB's and the column type from `sys.dm_exec_describe_first_result_set` / `pg_typeof`, including `CAST(CAST(0.1 AS FLOAT) AS DOUBLE)` = 0.100000001 and 16777217 as 16777216, and a `CREATE TABLE` of eight FLOAT/REAL/FLOAT4/FLOAT(24)/DOUBLE/FLOAT8/FLOAT(53)/FLOAT(25) columns whose types are read back from `sys.dm_exec_describe_first_result_set` and `format_type`: `real` x4 and `float` / `double precision` x4).
- **Found, not fixed (a function's result type with a REAL argument, not the type written; OPEN-ITEMS W-11)**: on SQL Server `abs`, `ceil`, `floor` and `sign` of a REAL answer FLOAT (so `abs(CAST(1.005 AS FLOAT))` shows 1.004999995 where DuckDB shows 1.005) and `%` on a REAL is error 8117; on PostgreSQL `ceil`, `floor` and `sign` answer double precision and `real % real` does not exist. These were in the first version of the conformance list and taken out; they are measured, not covered.
- **Verified** on SQL Server 2022 and PostgreSQL 17 (docker): as above; the unit suite (2719 passed), the conformance groups quick (76 passed, 12 skipped), templates plus release (15 passed) and apply (186 passed, 27 skipped, 1 failed: `OdbcOriginConformanceTests.A_statement_that_runs_past_its_timeout_is_stopped_by_the_driver`, because this machine has no unixODBC `libodbc.so.2`; not related to this change). **Not verified**: Fabric (never run; its matrix and rule behavior follow SQL Server's writer, and the text tests cover it), SQL Server 2025, FLOAT values through a real `apply` of a model with a FLOAT column (the DDL path was exact before and is unchanged).

## 180. VALUES cells are transpiled as a select list: interval cells need no SELECT per row (OPEN-ITEMS W-10(b)); lateral lists (W-3)

### Part 1: interval cells

- **Reproduced** (the pinned polyglot `native-0a7a1a77`, `values-selects` off): `SELECT * FROM (VALUES (DATE '2024-01-31' + INTERVAL 1 MONTH, 1), ...)` lowers to `CAST((DATE '2024-01-31' + INTERVAL 1 MONTH) AS TIMESTAMP)` in the cell; `interval-dateadd` does not fire (a single plain unit is left to the transpile, which writes DATEADD in a select list) and the transpile writes `CAST((CAST('2024-01-31' AS DATE) + INTERVAL '1' MONTH) AS DATETIME2)`, which SQL Server does not parse. `date + 3` fires `date-plus-days` and still reaches the engine as `CAST(... + INTERVAL (3) DAY AS DATE)`; `timestamp + INTERVAL '1 day 2 hours'` fires `interval-dateadd`, whose output is again `+ INTERVAL '1' DAY` in the cell; `date - INTERVAL` the same. `INTERVAL 1 DAY * 3` is not read by any rule and reaches the engine as an interval in a select list too (a gap of its own, not of VALUES).
- **The cause is not "DATEADD only in a select list"**: the transpile rewrites `d + INTERVAL n UNIT` to DATEADD in WHERE, ON, GROUP BY, ORDER BY, CASE, a derived table and INSERT ... VALUES. It does **not** rewrite any expression in the cells of a VALUES list that is a table in FROM; there it changes only the types. A scan of 80 expressions (select list against VALUES cell, SQL Server and PostgreSQL) found the same for `ceil` (CEIL, not T-SQL), `ln`, `||`, `repeat`, `last_day`, `date_diff('day', ...)`, `date - date`, `current_date`, `starts_with`, `regexp_matches`, `/` by a constant, `CAST(x AS VARCHAR)` (30 characters instead of MAX) and `json_extract_string`: all of them reached the engine as DuckDB wrote them. So the owner's point holds (SQL Server takes DATEADD in a VALUES row), and the defect was wider than intervals: any function in a cell. The rules of the target step were right; what was missing was the transpile.
- **Built**: `TargetRules.Transpile` replaces `Polyglot.TranspileOne` in `LoadRenderer` (both places), `Planner` and the test pipelines. A cell that is more than a constant becomes a placeholder column `ddb_cell_N`, the distinct cells are transpiled together as the select list of one query (`SELECT e0 AS ddb_c0, e1 AS ddb_c1, ...`), and the text goes back where the placeholders are; a statement with no `VALUES` is passed through as before, and a list of constants is byte-for-byte what it was. The rule `values-selects` (rule, catalog entry, DESIGN text) is **removed**: nothing needs it. `values-chunks` splits a long interval list like any list. `interval-dateadd` reads `interval * n` and `n * interval` for a whole-number constant (each part times n). A BIGINT cell that is a calculation (`length`, `year`, days between) is cast to BIGINT (INT on SQL Server, NUMERIC on PostgreSQL otherwise). `JsonNode.Parse` of the AST allows 4096 levels (a query with enough joins nests past the default 64 once wrapped; found by the adventureworks template).
- **Tests, three ways**: unit text (`ValuesListTests`: 12 interval-cell queries on SQL Server and Fabric with no INTERVAL, no UNION ALL and a constructor; exact DATEADD text; 1001 and 5000 rows chunked 2 and 5 ways with one DATEADD per cell; nine expression cells against the select-list translation; constants unchanged; equal cells transpiled once); the differential against DuckDB (every interval-cell query: the lowered query's rows, names and types equal the original's, `ValuesListTests.Queries` and the new theory); and real engines (`ValuesListConformance`, SQL Server 2022 and PostgreSQL 17, rows, names and types against DuckDB's: five interval shapes including `interval * n`, an interval join side, the function cells, 1200 rows of function cells, and 1001 and 5000 rows of interval cells).
- **Measured, SQL Server 2022 (docker), lists of date + interval, date + days and timestamp + hours cells, 4 columns, each text run three times with a different trailing comment so every run compiles**: 1001 rows, new (2 constructors, 1 UNION ALL) 80 to 113 ms against 79 to 91 ms for the old one-SELECT-per-row form; 5000 rows, new (5 constructors) 540 to 579 ms against 501 to 520 ms for the old (5000 UNION ALL branches; it compiled and returned the same rows, no nesting error). The new form is about 5 percent slower at 5000 rows, the text is a fifth longer, and a second run is no faster (nothing is cached for a changed comment): the cost is the cell text, not the branch count. Pipeline cost: rendering 5000 rows with expression cells takes 3.5 s against 1.15 s with constant cells (lowering and the cell batch). PostgreSQL: 1001 rows 16 to 52 ms, 5000 rows 74 to 86 ms (unchanged by this entry).

### Part 2: lateral VALUES lists

- **DuckDB's plan** for `SELECT t.id, v.x, v.y FROM t, LATERAL (VALUES (t.a, 1), (t.a + 1, 2)) AS v(x, y)` (unoptimized, DuckDB 1.5.4; the same on the 2.0 preview): `PROJECTION` over `DELIM_JOIN` (type INNER, `duplicate_eliminated_columns` = `t.a`, condition `a IS NOT DISTINCT FROM a`) of `GET t` and `PROJECTION` over `EXPRESSION_GET`, whose child is `DELIM_GET` (not the dummy scan of a constant list). Its cells read the outer value as `BOUND_REF` into the delim columns, and the **last K columns of the EXPRESSION_GET carry the K outer values** (a `BOUND_REF` to delim column j in every row) for the join condition. `LEFT JOIN LATERAL ... ON TRUE` is the same with join type LEFT; a correlated list inside EXISTS or a scalar subquery is the same node under a MARK or SINGLE join. The lowerer already turned a DELIM_JOIN with an INNER or LEFT type into `CROSS JOIN LATERAL` / `LEFT JOIN LATERAL ... ON TRUE`; only `ValuesList` refused (any child other than the dummy scan, any reference).
- **Built**: `ValuesList` accepts a DELIM_GET child, lowers the cells against the outer values (`t.a` is written as the outer reference every other correlated subquery uses), writes only the author's columns, and gives the K carried columns the outer items so the join's plumbing finds them. Anything else under the list (a join, a filter, a subquery in a cell: `BOUND_SUBQUERY`) stays refused, DDB-324 with "a VALUES list with a subquery in a cell, or a lateral list with another operator under it". The SQL Server text is the owner's `CROSS APPLY (SELECT vals.col0 AS col0 ... FROM (VALUES (t.a, 1), ((t.a + 1), 2)) AS vals(col0, col1)) AS s1` (OUTER APPLY for the left join), PostgreSQL `CROSS JOIN LATERAL (...)` / `LEFT JOIN LATERAL (...) ON TRUE`; polyglot already makes the APPLY, and the cells are transpiled as a select list (part 1), so `t.d + INTERVAL 1 MONTH`, `||` and a boolean outer column (`t.f <> 0`) come out right. No new rule, no new diagnostic code; the matrix row `syntax.values_list` note says it (the matrix hash did not change, no golden changed).
- **A bug found on the way, in every lateral join, not only a list**: when the outer block is a derived table with a filter (`FROM (SELECT * FROM t WHERE id > 2) q, LATERAL (...)`), the lowerer wrapped the outer block after the lateral side was lowered, so the lateral side read `t.a` where `s1` was in scope (SQL Server: "The multi-part identifier could not be bound"; PostgreSQL: "missing FROM-clause entry"). `RebaseOuterReferences` now points the lateral side's references at the wrapped block's columns. Covered by `PlanLowererTests` (a `LATERAL (SELECT ...)` and a `LEFT JOIN LATERAL` over a filtered derived table, rows against DuckDB) and `ValuesListConformance`.
- **Tests, three ways**: unit text (`ValuesListTests`: exact lowered text, CROSS APPLY and OUTER APPLY on SQL Server and Fabric with no LATERAL left, LATERAL on PostgreSQL, a 2100-row lateral list chunked into three with the outer column in every cell, the refusals: a subquery cell and a TINYINT column); the differential against DuckDB (17 lateral queries on a table with NULLs and a table with no rows, rows, names and types of the lowered query equal the original's: several rows per outer row, NULL cells, `LEFT JOIN LATERAL ... ON TRUE`, two outer columns, text, decimal mix, chained lateral lists, GROUP BY over a lateral list, ORDER BY, EXISTS, scalar subquery, CTE, an outer table with zero rows with cross, left and count); real engines (`ValuesListConformance`, SQL Server 2022 and PostgreSQL 17, rows, names and types against DuckDB's: 24 lateral cases, the same shapes over the probe table with its NULLs, zero-row outer tables as a filtered derived table, dates and timestamps plus intervals in cells, a list of 1100 rows, a list joined to a constant list, an ordered `LIMIT 1` list, and the filtered-outer cases). The conformance type check now reads the types of a query that returns no rows from the null row of an outer join.
- **Not exact, stays refused (DDB-324 naming the reason)**: a lateral list with a subquery in a cell; a lateral join with a real ON condition (`JOIN LATERAL (...) ON v.x = t.id`; "a lateral join with a comparison", a refusal of the existing lateral code, which an INNER join could turn into a WHERE, not done); lists of the unsupported column types.
- **Verified** on SQL Server 2022 and PostgreSQL 17 (docker), and `ValuesListTests` on the DuckDB 2.0 preview (`scripts/test-duckdb-preview.sh`): all lateral tests pass; six text assertions of other tests differ only because 2.0 folds constants before the plan (`upper('a')` becomes `'A'`, a sum of intervals and `date - date` become constants), with equal rows. **Not verified**: Fabric (never run; its text is SQL Server's), SQL Server 2025, a lateral list in a model that is applied (the lowered text was rendered and run through the conformance harness, not through `apply`).
